We prove this approximation in what follows.

E.1
Decomposition

Define
\varepsilon^{\lambda}_{i}=Y_{i}-f_{\lambda}(X_{i})
and recall that

V_{i}=T_{\lambda}^{-1}\{(k_{X_{i}}\otimes k_{X_{i}}^{*})(f_{0}-f_{\lambda})+\varepsilon_{i}k_{X_{i}}\}=T_{\lambda}^{-1}\{Y_{i}-f_{\lambda}(X_{i})\}k_{X_{i}}=T_{\lambda}^{-1}\varepsilon^{\lambda}_{i}k_{X_{i}}

and that its feasible counterpart is

\hat{V_{i}}=\hat{T}_{\lambda}^{-1}\{(k_{X_{i}}\otimes k_{X_{i}}^{*})(f_{0}-\hat{f})+\varepsilon_{i}k_{X_{i}}\}=\hat{T}_{\lambda}^{-1}\{Y_{i}-\hat{f}(X_{i})\}k_{X_{i}}=\hat{T}_{\lambda}^{-1}\hat{\varepsilon}_{i}k_{X_{i}}.

To lighten notation, let

w_{ij}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\varepsilon^{\lambda}_{i}k_{X_{i}}-\varepsilon^{\lambda}_{j}k_{X_{j}}),\quad\hat{w}_{ij}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\hat{\varepsilon}_{i}k_{X_{i}}-\hat{\varepsilon}_{j}k_{X_{j}})

so that the comparison of interest is

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}h_{ij}\hat{T}_{\lambda}^{-1}\hat{w}_{ij}\approx\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}h_{ij}T^{-1}_{\lambda}w_{ij}.

Lemma E.1
.

We have that

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}h_{ij}\frac{\hat{V}_{i}-\hat{V}_{j}}{\sqrt{2}}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}h_{ij}\frac{V_{i}-V_{j}}{\sqrt{2}}=\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}

where

\displaystyle\Delta_{1}

\displaystyle=(\hat{T}_{\lambda}^{-1}-T_{\lambda}^{-1})\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\hat{w}_{ij}h_{ij}\right),\quad\Delta_{2}=T_{\lambda}^{-1}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}(\hat{w}_{ij}-w_{ij})h_{ij}\right\}.

Proof.

Write

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}h_{ij}\frac{\hat{V}_{i}-\hat{V}_{j}}{\sqrt{2}}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}h_{ij}\frac{V_{i}-V_{j}}{\sqrt{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}h_{ij}\hat{T}_{\lambda}^{-1}\hat{w}_{ij}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}h_{ij}T_{\lambda}^{-1}w_{ij}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}h_{ij}\{\hat{T}_{\lambda}^{-1}\hat{w}_{ij}-T_{\lambda}^{-1}w_{ij}\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}h_{ij}\{\hat{T}_{\lambda}^{-1}\hat{w}_{ij}\pm T_{\lambda}^{-1}\hat{w}_{ij}-T_{\lambda}^{-1}w_{ij}\}.

∎

E.2
Finite sample rate

The following is a special case of
Fischer and Steinwart, 2020
. We give a self-contained proof, as the version needed here is an easy consequence of our Proposition
B.3
.

Lemma E.2

(cf.
Fischer and Steinwart, 2020
)
.

Suppose
\|T_{\lambda}(\hat{T}-T)\|_{\mathrm{HS}}\leq\delta\leq\frac{1}{2}
and
\left\lVert T_{\lambda}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}[k_{X_{i}}\varepsilon_{i}]\right\rVert\leq\gamma
. Then
we have

\lVert\hat{f}-f_{\lambda}\rVert\leq 2(\gamma+\delta\lVert f_{0}\rVert).

Proof.

Recall Proposition
B.3
, which says that if

\|T_{\lambda}(\hat{T}-T)\|_{\mathrm{HS}}\leq\delta\leq\frac{1}{2}
and
\left\lVert T_{\lambda}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}[k_{X_{i}}\varepsilon_{i}]\right\rVert\leq\gamma
, then

\hat{f}-f_{\lambda}=T_{\lambda}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}[k_{X_{i}}\varepsilon_{i}]+T_{\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{T}-T)(f_{0}-f_{\lambda})+u

for some
u
with

\left\lVert u\right\rVert\leq 2\delta\left(\gamma+\delta\|f_{0}-f_{\lambda}\|\right).

Under these events, by the triangle inequality,

\lVert\hat{f}-f_{\lambda}\rVert\leq\gamma+\delta\lVert f_{0}-f_{\lambda}\rVert+2\delta(\gamma+\delta\lVert f_{0}-f_{\lambda}\rVert).

Since
f_{0}-f_{\lambda}=(I-T_{\lambda}^{-1}T)f_{0}
and

0\preceq(I-T_{\lambda}^{-1}T)\preceq I,
it further follows that

\lVert\hat{f}-f_{\lambda}\rVert\leq\gamma+\delta\lVert f_{0}\rVert+2\delta(\gamma+\delta\lVert f_{0}\rVert)\leq 2(\gamma+\delta\lVert f_{0}\rVert),

where we again used our assumption that
\delta\leq\frac{1}{2}
.
∎

E.3
First term

We initially focus on

\Delta_{1}=(\hat{T}_{\lambda}^{-1}-T_{\lambda}^{-1})v
where
v=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\hat{w}_{ij}h_{ij}.

Lemma E.3
.

Suppose
\|T_{\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{T}-T)\|_{\mathrm{HS}}\leq\delta\leq\frac{1}{2}
. Then

\|\Delta_{1}\|\leq 2\delta\|T_{\lambda}^{-1}v\|.

Proof.

Recall Lemma
B.2
:

(\hat{T}_{\lambda}^{-1}-T_{\lambda}^{-1})v=A_{1}v+A_{2}T_{\lambda}^{-1}v+A_{3}T^{-1}_{\lambda}v

where

\|A_{1}\|_{\mathrm{HS}}\leq\frac{\delta^{k}}{\lambda},\quad\|A_{2}\|_{\mathrm{HS}}\leq\delta,\quad\|A_{3}\|_{\mathrm{HS}}\leq\frac{\delta^{2}}{1-\delta}.

Therefore

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{1}\|

\displaystyle=\|(\hat{T}_{\lambda}^{-1}-T_{\lambda}^{-1})v\|

\displaystyle\leq\|A_{1}\|_{\mathrm{HS}}\cdot\|v\|+\|A_{2}\|_{\mathrm{HS}}\cdot\|T_{\lambda}^{-1}v\|+\|A_{3}\|_{\mathrm{HS}}\cdot\|T_{\lambda}^{-1}v\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\delta^{k}}{\lambda}\|v\|+\left(\delta+\frac{\delta^{2}}{1-\delta}\right)\|T_{\lambda}^{-1}v\|

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta^{k}}{\lambda}\|v\|+\frac{\delta}{1-\delta}\|T_{\lambda}^{-1}v\|.

Taking the limit as
k\uparrow\infty
removes the first term. Then
\delta\leq 1/2
implies
\frac{1}{1-\delta}\leq 2
so that

\frac{\delta}{1-\delta}\|T_{\lambda}^{-1}v\|\leq 2\delta\|T_{\lambda}^{-1}v\|.

∎

Lemma E.4
.

Suppose
\|f-f_{0}\|\leq z_{0}
and
\varepsilon_{i}\leq\bar{\sigma}
. Conditional on the data, w.p.
1-\eta
,

\left\lVert T_{\lambda}^{-1}v\right\rVert\leq 8\log(4/\eta)(\bar{\sigma}\kappa+\kappa^{2}z_{0})\left[\sqrt{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}+\left\{\frac{1}{n^{1/2}\lambda}\vee\frac{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)^{1/4}}{n^{1/4}\lambda^{1/2}}\right\}\right].

Proof.

We proceed in steps.

1.

Borell’s inequality (Lemma
D.5
). Since
Z=T_{\lambda}^{-1}v
is Gaussian
we know that with probability at least
1-\eta
,

\left\lVert Z\right\rVert\leq\left(1+\sqrt{2\log(1/\eta)}\right)\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left\lVert Z\right\rVert^{2}}.

Therefore it suffices to control
\mathbb{E}\left\lVert T_{\lambda}^{-1}v\right\rVert^{2}
.
Note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{h}\left\lVert T_{\lambda}^{-1}v\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\|T_{\lambda}^{-1}\hat{w}_{ij}\|^{2}

where

\hat{w}_{ij}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\hat{\varepsilon}_{i}k_{X_{i}}-\hat{\varepsilon}_{j}k_{X_{j}}).

2.

Single sum representation. Note that

\displaystyle\|T_{\lambda}^{-1}\hat{w}_{ij}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|T_{\lambda}^{-1}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\hat{\varepsilon}_{i}k_{X_{i}}-\hat{\varepsilon}_{j}k_{X_{j}})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left\|T_{\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{\varepsilon}_{i}k_{X_{i}}-\hat{\varepsilon}_{j}k_{X_{j}})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|T_{\lambda}^{-1}\hat{\varepsilon}_{i}k_{X_{i}}\|^{2}+\|T_{\lambda}^{-1}\hat{\varepsilon}_{j}k_{X_{j}}\|^{2}

by Cauchy-Schwartz. Hence

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\|T_{\lambda}^{-1}\hat{w}_{ij}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\|T_{\lambda}^{-1}\hat{\varepsilon}_{i}k_{X_{i}}\|^{2}+\|T_{\lambda}^{-1}\hat{\varepsilon}_{j}k_{X_{j}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|T_{\lambda}^{-1}\hat{\varepsilon}_{i}k_{X_{i}}\|^{2}=2\mathbb{E}_{n}[\xi_{i}]

where
\xi_{i}=\|T_{\lambda}^{-1}\hat{\varepsilon}_{i}k_{X_{i}}\|^{2}
.

3.

Concentration. We control
\xi_{i}
,
\mathbb{E}[\xi_{i}]
and hence

\mathbb{E}[\xi_{i}^{2}]\leq\xi_{i}\mathbb{E}(\xi_{i}).

Then we apply Bernstein’s inequality (Lemma
H.2
). To begin, write

\displaystyle\hat{\varepsilon}_{i}k_{X_{i}}

\displaystyle=\{Y_{i}-\hat{f}(X_{i})\}k_{X_{i}}=\{\varepsilon_{i}+f_{0}(X_{i})-\hat{f}(X_{i})\}k_{X_{i}}=\varepsilon_{i}k_{X_{i}}+T_{i}(f_{0}-\hat{f}).

Hence

\displaystyle\xi_{i}

\displaystyle=\|T_{\lambda}^{-1}\hat{\varepsilon}_{i}k_{X_{i}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\|T_{\lambda}^{-1}\{\varepsilon_{i}k_{X_{i}}+T_{i}(f_{0}-\hat{f})\}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}(\bar{\sigma}\kappa+\kappa^{2}\|\hat{f}-f_{0}\|)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}(\bar{\sigma}\kappa+\kappa^{2}z_{0})^{2}.

Moreover

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\xi_{i}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\|T_{\lambda}^{-1}\hat{\varepsilon}_{i}k_{X_{i}}\|^{2}\leq 2\mathbb{E}\|T_{\lambda}^{-1}\varepsilon_{i}k_{X_{i}}\|^{2}+2\mathbb{E}\|T_{\lambda}^{-1}T_{i}(f_{0}-\hat{f})\|^{2}.

In the first term

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|T_{\lambda}^{-1}\varepsilon_{i}k_{X_{i}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\int\left[\varepsilon_{i}^{2}\langle k_{X_{i}},T_{\lambda}^{-2}k_{X_{i}}\rangle\right]\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle\leq\bar{\sigma}^{2}\int\left[\langle k_{X_{i}},T_{\lambda}^{-2}k_{X_{i}}\rangle\right]\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle=\bar{\sigma}^{2}\int\left[\tr(T_{\lambda}^{-2}T_{i})\right]\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle=\bar{\sigma}^{2}\tr(T_{\lambda}^{-2}T)

\displaystyle=\bar{\sigma}^{2}\mathfrak{n}(\lambda).

In the second term

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|T_{\lambda}^{-1}T_{i}(f_{0}-\hat{f})\}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\|T_{\lambda}^{-1}k_{X_{i}}\{f_{0}(X_{i})-\hat{f}(X_{i})\}\|^{2}\leq\|f_{0}-\hat{f}\|^{2}_{\infty}\mathbb{E}\|T_{\lambda}^{-1}k_{X_{i}}\|^{2}.

Note that, as argued above,

\|f_{0}-\hat{f}\|^{2}_{\infty}\leq\kappa^{2}\|f_{0}-\hat{f}\|^{2},\quad\mathbb{E}\|T_{\lambda}^{-1}k_{X_{i}}\|^{2}=\int[\langle k_{X_{i}},T_{\lambda}^{-2}k_{X_{i}}\rangle]\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}=\mathfrak{n}(\lambda).

In summary, the second term is bounded as

\mathbb{E}\|T_{\lambda}^{-1}T_{i}(f_{0}-\hat{f})\}\|^{2}\leq\kappa^{2}\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)\|f_{0}-\hat{f}\|^{2}

and hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\xi_{i}]

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(\bar{\sigma}^{2}\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)+\kappa^{2}\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)\|f_{0}-\hat{f}\|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=2\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)\left(\bar{\sigma}^{2}+\kappa^{2}\|f_{0}-\hat{f}\|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)\left(\bar{\sigma}^{2}+\kappa^{2}z_{0}^{2}\right).

It follows that

\mathbb{E}[\xi_{i}^{2}]\leq 2\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)\left(\bar{\sigma}^{2}+\kappa^{2}z_{0}^{2}\right)\cdot\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}(\bar{\sigma}\kappa+\kappa^{2}z_{0})^{2}.

Therefore by Bernstein’s inequality (Lemma
H.2
), w.p.
1-\eta

|\mathbb{E}_{n}[\xi_{i}]-\mathbb{E}[\xi_{i}]|\leq 2\ln(2/\eta)\left\{\frac{2}{n\lambda^{2}}(\bar{\sigma}\kappa+\kappa^{2}z_{0})^{2}\vee\sqrt{2\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)\left(\bar{\sigma}^{2}+\kappa^{2}z_{0}^{2}\right)\cdot\frac{1}{n\lambda^{2}}(\bar{\sigma}\kappa+\kappa^{2}z_{0})^{2}}\right\}.

In particular,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{n}[\xi_{i}]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}[\xi_{i}]+2\ln(2/\eta)\left\{\frac{2}{n\lambda^{2}}(\bar{\sigma}\kappa+\kappa^{2}z_{0})^{2}\vee\sqrt{2\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)\left(\bar{\sigma}^{2}+\kappa^{2}z_{0}^{2}\right)\cdot\frac{1}{n\lambda^{2}}(\bar{\sigma}\kappa+\kappa^{2}z_{0})^{2}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)\left(\bar{\sigma}^{2}+\kappa^{2}z_{0}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+2\ln(2/\eta)\left\{\frac{2}{n\lambda^{2}}(\bar{\sigma}\kappa+\kappa^{2}z_{0})^{2}\vee\sqrt{2\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)\left(\bar{\sigma}^{2}+\kappa^{2}z_{0}^{2}\right)\cdot\frac{1}{n\lambda^{2}}(\bar{\sigma}\kappa+\kappa^{2}z_{0})^{2}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq 4\ln(2/\eta)(\bar{\sigma}\kappa+\kappa^{2}z_{0})^{2}\left[\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)+\left\{\frac{1}{n\lambda^{2}}\vee\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}{n\lambda^{2}}}\right\}\right].

4.

Collecting results. We have shown by concentration that, w.p.
1-\eta
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{h}\left\lVert T_{\lambda}^{-1}v\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\|T_{\lambda}^{-1}\hat{w}_{ij}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}_{n}[\xi_{i}]

\displaystyle\leq R:=8\ln(2/\eta)(\bar{\sigma}\kappa+\kappa^{2}z_{0})^{2}\left[\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)+\left\{\frac{1}{n\lambda^{2}}\vee\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}{n\lambda^{2}}}\right\}\right].

Therefore by Borell’s inequality, w.p.
1-2\eta

\displaystyle\|T_{\lambda}v\|

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+\sqrt{2\log(1/\eta)}\right)\sqrt{R}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+\sqrt{2\log(1/\eta)}\right)\sqrt{8}\ln(2/\eta)^{1/2}(\bar{\sigma}\kappa+\kappa^{2}z_{0})\left[\sqrt{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}+\left\{\frac{1}{n^{1/2}\lambda}\vee\frac{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)^{1/4}}{n^{1/4}\lambda^{1/2}}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 8\log(2/\eta)(\bar{\sigma}\kappa+\kappa^{2}z_{0})\left[\sqrt{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}+\left\{\frac{1}{n^{1/2}\lambda}\vee\frac{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)^{1/4}}{n^{1/4}\lambda^{1/2}}\right\}\right].

∎

Proposition E.5
.

Suppose that
\|T_{\lambda}(\hat{T}-T)\|_{\mathrm{HS}}\leq\delta\leq\frac{1}{2}
,
\|f-f_{0}\|\leq z_{0}
, and
\varepsilon\leq\bar{\sigma}
. It then holds w.p.
1-\eta
that

\|\Delta_{1}\|\leq 16\delta\log(4/\eta)(\bar{\sigma}\kappa+\kappa^{2}z_{0})\left[\sqrt{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}+\left\{\frac{1}{n^{1/2}\lambda}\vee\frac{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)^{1/4}}{n^{1/4}\lambda^{1/2}}\right\}\right].

Proof.

By Lemma
E.4
, w.p.
1-\eta

\left\lVert T_{\lambda}^{-1}v\right\rVert\leq 8\log(4/\eta)(\bar{\sigma}\kappa+\kappa^{2}z_{0})\left[\sqrt{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}+\left\{\frac{1}{n^{1/2}\lambda}\vee\frac{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)^{1/4}}{n^{1/4}\lambda^{1/2}}\right\}\right].

Therefore by Lemma
E.3
,

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{1}\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\delta\|T_{\lambda}^{-1}v\|

\displaystyle\leq 16\delta\log(4/\eta)(\bar{\sigma}\kappa+\kappa^{2}z_{0})\left[\sqrt{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}+\left\{\frac{1}{n^{1/2}\lambda}\vee\frac{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)^{1/4}}{n^{1/4}\lambda^{1/2}}\right\}\right].

∎

E.4
Second term

Next, we turn to

\Delta_{2}=T_{\lambda}^{-1}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}(\hat{w}_{ij}-w_{ij})h_{ij}\right\}.

Let
T_{i}=k_{X_{i}}\otimes k_{X_{i}}^{*}
.

Lemma E.6
.

\Delta_{2}=T_{\lambda}^{-1}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(T_{i}-T_{j})(f_{\lambda}-\hat{f})h_{ij}\right\}
.

Proof.

Write

\displaystyle\hat{w}_{ij}-w_{ij}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left[\{Y_{i}-\hat{f}(X_{i})\}k_{X_{i}}-\{Y_{j}-\hat{f}(X_{j})\}k_{X_{j}}\right]-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left[\{Y_{i}-f_{\lambda}(X_{i})\}k_{X_{i}}-\{Y_{j}-f_{\lambda}(X_{j})\}k_{X_{j}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left[\{f_{\lambda}(X_{i})-\hat{f}(X_{i})\}k_{X_{i}}-\{f_{\lambda}(X_{j})-\hat{f}(X_{j})\}k_{X_{j}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left[T_{i}\{f_{\lambda}-\hat{f}\}-T_{j}\{f_{\lambda}-\hat{f}\}\right].

∎

Our strategy is then to write

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{2}\|\leq\left\|T_{\lambda}^{-1}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(T_{i}-T_{j})h_{ij}\right\}\right\|_{op}\cdot\|\hat{f}-f_{\lambda}\|.

To control the second factor, we use Lemma
E.2
. For the former factor, we have the following.

Lemma E.7
.

With probability at least
1-\eta
, we have

\left\lVert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i,j}^{n}T_{\lambda}^{-1}\left(\frac{T_{i}-T_{j}}{\sqrt{2}}\right)h_{ij}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}\leq 8\kappa^{2}\ln(4/\eta)\left[\sqrt{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}\lambda}\vee\frac{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)^{1/4}}{n^{1/4}\lambda^{1/2}}\right].

Proof.

We proceed in steps.

1.

Second moment. Consider

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{h}\left\lVert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i,j}^{n}T_{\lambda}^{-1}\left(\frac{T_{i}-T_{j}}{\sqrt{2}}\right)h_{ij}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i,j}^{n}\left\lVert T_{\lambda}^{-1}\left(\frac{T_{i}-T_{j}}{\sqrt{2}}\right)\right\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2n^{2}}\sum_{i,j}^{n}\left\lVert B_{i}-B_{j}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}^{2}

where

B_{i}=T_{\lambda}^{-1}T_{i}
. By triangle inequality and AM-GM inequality,

\left\lVert B_{i}-B_{j}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}^{2}\leq 2(\left\lVert B_{i}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}^{2}+\left\lVert B_{j}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}^{2}).

Therefore

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2n^{2}}\sum_{i,j}^{n}\left\lVert B_{i}-B_{j}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i,j}^{n}\left\lVert B_{i}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}^{2}+\left\lVert B_{j}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}^{2}=\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\lVert B_{i}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}^{2}.

In summary

\mathbb{E}_{h}\left\lVert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i,j}^{n}T_{\lambda}^{-1}\left(\frac{T_{i}-T_{j}}{\sqrt{2}}\right)h_{ij}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}^{2}\leq\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\lVert B_{i}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}^{2}.

2.

Concentration. Next we bound the sample mean
\mathbb{E}_{n}[\xi_{i}]
where
\xi_{i}=\left\lVert B_{i}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}^{2}.

Note that

\xi_{i}=\left\lVert B_{i}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}^{2}=\left\lVert T_{\lambda}^{-1}T_{i}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}^{2}\leq\frac{\kappa^{4}}{\lambda^{2}}.

Moreover

\mathbb{E}(\xi_{i}^{2})\leq\xi_{i}\mathbb{E}(\xi_{i})\leq\frac{\kappa^{4}}{\lambda^{2}}\mathbb{E}(\xi_{i}).

Within the final expression,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(\xi_{i})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}(\left\lVert T_{\lambda}^{-1}T_{i}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}^{2})

\displaystyle=\int\tr(T_{i}T_{\lambda}^{-2}T_{i})\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}(x)

\displaystyle\leq\kappa^{2}\int\tr(T_{\lambda}^{-2}T_{i})\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}(x)

\displaystyle=\kappa^{2}\tr(T_{\lambda}^{-2}T)

\displaystyle=\kappa^{2}\mathfrak{n}(\lambda).

Hence

\mathbb{E}(\xi_{i}^{2})\leq\frac{\kappa^{6}}{\lambda^{2}}\mathfrak{n}(\lambda).

Therefore by Bernstein’s inequality (Lemma
H.2
), w.p.
1-\eta

|\mathbb{E}_{n}[\xi_{i}]-\mathbb{E}[\xi_{i}]|\leq 2\ln(2/\eta)\left\{\frac{2\kappa^{4}}{n\lambda^{2}}\vee\sqrt{\frac{\kappa^{6}\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}{n\lambda^{2}}}\right\}.

In particular,

\mathbb{E}_{n}[\xi_{i}]\leq\mathbb{E}[\xi_{i}]+2\ln(2/\eta)\left\{\frac{2\kappa^{4}}{n\lambda^{2}}\vee\sqrt{\frac{\kappa^{6}\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}{n\lambda^{2}}}\right\}\leq\kappa^{2}\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)+2\ln(2/\eta)\left\{\frac{2\kappa^{4}}{n\lambda^{2}}\vee\sqrt{\frac{\kappa^{6}\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}{n\lambda^{2}}}\right\}.

Thus w.p.
1-\eta

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{h}\left\lVert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i,j}^{n}T_{\lambda}^{-1}\left(\frac{T_{i}-T_{j}}{\sqrt{2}}\right)h_{ij}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}^{2}&\leq\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\lVert B_{i}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}^{2}\\
&\leq R\coloneqq 2\kappa^{2}\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)+4\ln(2/\eta)\left\{\frac{2\kappa^{4}}{n\lambda^{2}}\vee\sqrt{\frac{\kappa^{6}\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}{n\lambda^{2}}}\right\}.\end{split}

3.

Borell’s inequality (Lemma
D.5
). Since
Z=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i,j}^{n}T_{\lambda}^{-1}\left(\frac{T_{i}-T_{j}}{\sqrt{2}}\right)h_{ij}
is Gaussian, we know that with probability at least
1-\eta
,

\left\lVert Z\right\rVert\leq\left(1+\sqrt{2\log(1/\eta)}\right)\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left\lVert Z\right\rVert^{2}}.

Therefore it suffices to use the high probability bound
\mathbb{E}\left\lVert Z\right\rVert^{2}_{\mathrm{HS}}\leq R
derived above. In particular, w.p.
1-2\eta

\displaystyle\quad\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i,j}^{n}T_{\lambda}^{-1}\left(\frac{T_{i}-T_{j}}{\sqrt{2}}\right)h_{ij}\right\|_{\mathrm{HS}}

\displaystyle\leq\{1+\sqrt{2\ln(1/\eta)}\}R^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\{1+\sqrt{2\ln(1/\eta)}\}\left[2\kappa^{2}\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)+4\ln(2/\eta)\left\{\frac{2\kappa^{4}}{n\lambda^{2}}\vee\sqrt{\frac{\kappa^{6}\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}{n\lambda^{2}}}\right\}\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\{1+\sqrt{2\ln(1/\eta)}\}\left[\sqrt{2}\kappa\sqrt{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}+2\ln(2/\eta)^{1/2}\left\{\frac{\sqrt{2}\kappa^{2}}{\sqrt{n}\lambda}\vee\frac{\kappa^{3/2}\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)^{1/4}}{n^{1/4}\lambda^{1/2}}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 8\ln(2/\eta)\left[\kappa\sqrt{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}+\left\{\frac{\kappa^{2}}{\sqrt{n}\lambda}\vee\frac{\kappa^{3/2}\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)^{1/4}}{n^{1/4}\lambda^{1/2}}\right\}\right].

∎

Proposition E.8
.

It holds w.p.
1-\eta
that

\|\Delta_{2}\|\leq 8\kappa^{2}\ln(4/\eta)\left[\sqrt{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}+\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}\lambda}\vee\frac{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)^{1/4}}{n^{1/4}\lambda^{1/2}}\right\}\right]\cdot\|\hat{f}-f_{\lambda}\|.

Proof.

By Lemmas
E.6
and
E.7
, it holds w.p.
1-\eta
that

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{2}\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|T_{\lambda}^{-1}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(T_{i}-T_{j})h_{ij}\right\}\right\|_{\mathrm{HS}}\cdot\|\hat{f}-f_{\lambda}\|

\displaystyle\leq 8\kappa^{2}\ln(4/\eta)\left[\sqrt{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}+\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}\lambda}\vee\frac{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)^{1/4}}{n^{1/4}\lambda^{1/2}}\right\}\right]\cdot\|\hat{f}-f_{\lambda}\|.

∎

E.5
Main result

Theorem E.9
.

Suppose that
n\geq 16\kappa^{2}\ln(4/\eta)^{2}[\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)\vee\lambda^{-1}]
and
\varepsilon_{i}\leq\bar{\sigma}
. It then holds w.p.
1-\eta
that

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}h_{ij}\frac{\hat{V}_{i}-\hat{V}_{j}}{\sqrt{2}}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}h_{ij}\frac{V_{i}-V_{j}}{\sqrt{2}}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 96\kappa^{2}[\bar{\sigma}+\kappa\|f_{0}\|]\ln(16/\eta)^{2}\left\{\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}{n}}\vee\frac{2\kappa}{n\lambda}\right\}\cdot\left[\sqrt{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}+\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}\lambda}\vee\frac{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)^{1/4}}{n^{1/4}\lambda^{1/2}}\right\}\right].

Proof.

By Lemma
E.1
as well as Propositions
E.5
and
E.8
, w.p.
1-2\eta

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}h_{ij}\frac{\hat{V}_{i}-\hat{V}_{j}}{\sqrt{2}}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}h_{ij}\frac{V_{i}-V_{j}}{\sqrt{2}}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\Delta_{1}\|+\|\Delta_{2}\|

\displaystyle\leq 16\delta(\bar{\sigma}\kappa+\kappa^{2}z_{0})\log(4/\eta)\left[\sqrt{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}+\left\{\frac{1}{n^{1/2}\lambda}\vee\frac{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)^{1/4}}{n^{1/4}\lambda^{1/2}}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+8\kappa^{2}\|\hat{f}-f_{\lambda}\|\log(4/\eta)\left[\sqrt{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}+\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}\lambda}\vee\frac{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)^{1/4}}{n^{1/4}\lambda^{1/2}}\right\}\right].

Therefore it suffices to control

16\delta(\bar{\sigma}\kappa+\kappa^{2}z_{0})
and
8\kappa^{2}\|\hat{f}-f_{\lambda}\|.

Recall that, as argued in Lemma
E.2
, when
\delta\leq 1/2
,

\lVert\hat{f}-f_{\lambda}\rVert\leq 2(\gamma+\delta\lVert f_{0}\rVert),

so that

8\kappa^{2}\|\hat{f}-f_{\lambda}\|\leq 16\kappa^{2}(\gamma+\delta\lVert f_{0}\rVert)=16\kappa^{2}\gamma+16\kappa^{2}\delta\lVert f_{0}\rVert.

Moreover, as argued in Lemma
E.2

\displaystyle z_{0}

\displaystyle=\lVert\hat{f}-f_{\lambda}\rVert+\lVert f_{\lambda}-f_{0}\rVert\leq\lVert\hat{f}-f_{\lambda}\rVert+\lVert f_{0}\rVert\leq 2(\gamma+\delta\lVert f_{0}\rVert)+\lVert f_{0}\rVert\leq 2(\gamma+\|f_{0}\|)

so that

\displaystyle 16\delta(\bar{\sigma}\kappa+\kappa^{2}z_{0})

\displaystyle\leq 16\delta\{\bar{\sigma}\kappa+2\kappa^{2}(\gamma+\|f_{0}\|)\}

\displaystyle=16\delta\bar{\sigma}\kappa+32\delta\kappa^{2}\gamma+32\delta\kappa^{2}\|f_{0}\|

\displaystyle\leq 16\delta\bar{\sigma}\kappa+16\kappa^{2}\gamma+32\delta\kappa^{2}\|f_{0}\|.

Therefore

\displaystyle 8\kappa^{2}\|\hat{f}-f_{\lambda}\|+16\delta(\bar{\sigma}\kappa+\kappa^{2}z_{0})

\displaystyle\leq 16\kappa^{2}\gamma+16\kappa^{2}\delta\lVert f_{0}\rVert+16\delta\bar{\sigma}\kappa+16\kappa^{2}\gamma+32\delta\kappa^{2}\|f_{0}\|

\displaystyle=[32\kappa^{2}]\gamma+[48\kappa^{2}\|f_{0}\|+16\bar{\sigma}\kappa]\delta.

By combining Lemmas
H.4
and
H.5
, we have with probability at least
1-2\eta
that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}T_{\lambda}^{-1}\varepsilon_{i}k_{X_{i}}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq 2\bar{\sigma}\ln(2/\eta)\left\{\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}{n}}\vee\frac{2\kappa}{n\lambda}\right\}=\gamma

\displaystyle\left\lVert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}T_{\lambda}^{-1}(T_{i}-T)\right\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\kappa\ln(2/\eta)\left\{\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}{n}}\vee\frac{2\kappa}{n\lambda}\right\}=\delta.

Therefore

\displaystyle[32\kappa^{2}]\gamma+[48\kappa^{2}\|f_{0}\|+16\bar{\sigma}\kappa]\delta

\displaystyle\leq[32\kappa^{2}\bar{\sigma}+48\kappa^{3}\|f_{0}\|+16\kappa^{2}\bar{\sigma}]2\ln(2/\eta)\left\{\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}{n}}\vee\frac{2\kappa}{n\lambda}\right\}

\displaystyle=[48\kappa^{2}\bar{\sigma}+48\kappa^{3}\|f_{0}\|]2\ln(2/\eta)\left\{\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}{n}}\vee\frac{2\kappa}{n\lambda}\right\}

\displaystyle=96\kappa^{2}[\bar{\sigma}+\kappa\|f_{0}\|]\ln(2/\eta)\left\{\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}{n}}\vee\frac{2\kappa}{n\lambda}\right\}.

We conclude that w.p.
1-4\eta

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}h_{ij}\frac{\hat{V}_{i}-\hat{V}_{j}}{\sqrt{2}}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}h_{ij}\frac{V_{i}-V_{j}}{\sqrt{2}}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 96\kappa^{2}[\bar{\sigma}+\kappa\|f_{0}\|]\ln(4/\eta)^{2}\left\{\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}{n}}\vee\frac{2\kappa}{n\lambda}\right\}\cdot\left[\sqrt{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}+\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}\lambda}\vee\frac{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)^{1/4}}{n^{1/4}\lambda^{1/2}}\right\}\right].

Finally note that the condition on
n
ensures
\delta\leq 1/2
, as argued in the proof of Theorem
B.4
.
∎

Appendix F
Practitioner’s guide

The sketch in Section
6
has the step

\mathfrak{B}(x)\coloneqq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}h_{ij}\frac{\hat{V}_{i}-\hat{V}_{j}}{\sqrt{2}}=K_{x}(K+n\lambda)^{-1}\diag(\hat{}\varepsilon)\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(h^{\top}-h)\bm{1},

where
(K_{x})_{i}=k(x,X_{i})
,
K_{ij}=k(X_{i},X_{j})
,
\hat{}\varepsilon_{i}=Y_{i}-\hat{f}(X_{i})
,
\bm{1}_{i}=1
, and
h
is a matrix whose entries are i.i.d. standard Gaussians. We prove this equality in what follows.

F.1
Simulation

Our goal is to sample from the symmetrized empirical process. Let us construct the estimated residuals
\hat{}\varepsilon_{i}=Y_{i}-\hat{f}(X_{i})
, which are collected into the vector
\hat{}\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Let
h=(h_{ij})
be an
n\times n
matrix consisting of independent, standard Gaussian multipliers.

Theorem F.1
.

We have that

\mathfrak{B}(x)=K_{x}(K+n\lambda)^{-1}\diag(\hat{}\varepsilon)\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(h^{\top}-h)\bm{1}.

Proof.

We proceed in steps.

1.

Notation. To begin, write

\displaystyle\mathfrak{B}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}h_{ij}\frac{\hat{V}_{i}-\hat{V}_{j}}{\sqrt{2}}=\hat{T}_{\lambda}^{-1}\left[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left(\frac{k_{X_{i}}\hat{}\varepsilon_{i}-k_{X_{j}}\hat{}\varepsilon_{j}}{\sqrt{2}}\right)h_{ij}\right].

Focusing on the inner expression

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left(\frac{k_{X_{i}}\hat{}\varepsilon_{i}-k_{X_{j}}\hat{}\varepsilon_{j}}{\sqrt{2}}\right)h_{ij}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{X_{i}}\hat{}\varepsilon_{i}\sum_{j=1}^{n}h_{ij}-\sum_{j=1}^{n}k_{X_{j}}\hat{}\varepsilon_{j}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{ij}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{X_{i}}\hat{}\varepsilon_{i}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}h_{ij}-h_{ji}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\beta_{i}k_{X_{i}}

for
\beta=\diag(\hat{}\varepsilon)\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(h^{\top}-h)\bm{1}
. The argument above shows

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left(\frac{k_{X_{i}}\hat{}\varepsilon_{i}-k_{X_{j}}\hat{}\varepsilon_{j}}{\sqrt{2}}\right)h_{ij}=\Phi^{*}\beta,\quad\beta=\diag(\hat{}\varepsilon)\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(h^{\top}-h)\bm{1}

where
\Phi:H\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
is the sampling operator
\Phi:f\mapsto(\left\langle k_{X_{i}},\,f\right\rangle)_{i=1}^{n}
, so
\hat{T}=\Phi^{*}\Phi
.

2.

Sample representation. Therefore

\displaystyle\mathfrak{B}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{n}\Phi^{*}\Phi+\lambda\right)^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{n}\Phi^{*}\beta\right)=\left(\Phi^{*}\Phi+n\lambda\right)^{-1}\Phi^{*}\beta=\Phi^{*}(\Phi\Phi^{*}+n\lambda)^{-1}\beta

and hence

\mathfrak{B}(x)=K_{x}(K+n\lambda)^{-1}\beta.

∎

F.2
Inference

F.2.1
Fixed width

By simulation, we may sample from the distribution of

\textsc{M}=\sup_{x\in S}|\mathfrak{B}(x)|=\sup_{x\in S}|K_{x}(K+n\lambda)^{-1}\diag(\hat{}\varepsilon)\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(h^{\top}-h)\bm{1}|.

Let
\hat{\tau}_{\alpha}
denote the
\alpha
quantile of this distribution, which is computed by simulation of
h
. Then, we may construct a fixed-width
\alpha
-sensitivity band as the set

\hat{C}_{\alpha}=\left\{(s,t)\in S\times\mathbb{R}\,\middle|\,|t-\hat{f}(s)|\leq\hat{\tau}_{\alpha}\right\}.

F.2.2
Variable width

We may also wish to incorporate information about the pointwise variance of our process. To do this, we first compute the estimate

\hat{\sigma}^{2}(x)=n\left\lVert K_{x}(K+n\lambda)^{-1}\diag(\hat{}\varepsilon)\right\rVert^{2}_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}.

We then sample from the distribution of

\textsc{M}^{\prime}=\sup_{x\in S}\left|\frac{\mathfrak{B}(x)}{\hat{\sigma}(x)n^{-1/2}}\right|=\sup_{x\in S}\left|\frac{K_{x}(K+n\lambda)^{-1}\diag(\hat{}\varepsilon)\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(h^{\top}-h)\bm{1}}{\left\lVert K_{x}(K+n\lambda)^{-1}\diag(\hat{}\varepsilon)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}}\right|

to compute the
\alpha
quantile,
\hat{\tau}^{\prime}_{\alpha}
, again by simulation of
h
. Having done so, we may construct the variable width confidence band

\displaystyle\hat{C}^{\prime}_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\left\{(s,t)\in S\times\mathbb{R}\,\middle|\,|t-\hat{f}(s)|\leq\hat{\sigma}(x)n^{-1/2}\tau^{\prime}_{\alpha}\right\}

\displaystyle=\left\{(s,t)\in S\times\mathbb{R}\,\middle|\,|t-\hat{f}(s)|\leq\left\lVert K_{x}(K+n\lambda)^{-1}\diag(\hat{}\varepsilon)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\tau^{\prime}_{\alpha}\right\}.

The derivation of
\hat{\sigma}^{2}(x)
is as follows. Recall that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{B}(x)

\displaystyle=K_{x}(K+n\lambda)^{-1}\beta

\displaystyle\beta

\displaystyle=\diag(\hat{}\varepsilon)\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(h^{\top}-h)\bm{1}=\sqrt{n}\diag(\hat{}\varepsilon)q

\displaystyle q

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2n}}(h-h^{\top})\mathbf{1},\quad\mathrm{var}(q_{i})<1.

Proposition F.2

(Pointwise variance estimation in small samples)
.

Taking the expectation over randomness in
h
,

\mathbb{E}_{h}\{\mathfrak{B}(x)\}^{2}=nK_{x}(K+n\lambda)^{-1}\diag(\hat{}\varepsilon)(I-\mathbf{1}\mathbf{1}^{\top}/n)\diag(\hat{}\varepsilon)(K+n\lambda)^{-1}K_{x}^{\top}.

For large
n
,
\mathbf{1}\mathbf{1}^{\top}/n
is close to
\mathbf{0}\mathbf{0}^{\top}
, and hence
\mathbb{E}_{h}\{\mathfrak{B}(x)\}^{2}
is close to
\hat{\sigma}^{2}(x)
. For small samples, the more complicated expression may be preferred.

Proof.

Recall that

q_{i}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2n}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left(h_{ij}-h_{ji}\right),\quad q=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2n}}(h-h^{\top})\mathbf{1},\quad var(q_{i})<1.

We proceed in steps, suppressing the subscript
h
which mean integrating over
h
.

1.

For diagonal terms, fix
i
and write

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(q_{i}^{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left\{\left(h_{ij}-h_{ji}\right)\left(h_{ik}-h_{ki}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left(h_{ij}h_{ik}-h_{ij}h_{ki}-h_{ji}h_{ik}+h_{ji}h_{ki}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(1_{j=k}-1_{i=j=k}-1_{i=j=k}+1_{j=k}\right)

\displaystyle=1-1/n.

2.

For off diagonal terms, fix
i\neq\ell
and write

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(q_{i}q_{\ell})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left\{\left(h_{ij}-h_{ji}\right)\left(h_{\ell k}-h_{k\ell}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left(h_{ij}h_{\ell k}-h_{ij}h_{k\ell}-h_{ji}h_{\ell k}+h_{ji}h_{k\ell}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(1_{i=\ell,j=k}-1_{i=k,j=\ell}-1_{j=\ell,i=k}+1_{j=k,i=\ell}\right)

\displaystyle=-1/n.

3.

We conclude that
\mathbb{E}(qq^{\top})=I-\mathbf{1}\mathbf{1}^{\top}/n
.

4.

Collecting results, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\{\mathfrak{B}(x)\}^{2}

\displaystyle=K_{x}(K+n\lambda)^{-1}\mathbb{E}(\beta\beta^{\top})(K+n\lambda)^{-1}K_{x}^{\top}

where

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(\beta\beta^{\top})

\displaystyle=n\diag(\hat{}\varepsilon)\mathbb{E}(qq^{\top})\diag(\hat{}\varepsilon)=n\diag(\hat{}\varepsilon)(I-\mathbf{1}\mathbf{1}^{\top}/n)\diag(\hat{}\varepsilon).

∎

Appendix G
Uniform confidence bands

G.1
Incremental factor approach

Lemma G.1

(One-sided error bound)
.

Let
V,W
be random variables such that
\mathbb{P}(|V-W|>r_{1}|\mathcal{A})\leq r_{2}
for some
r_{1},r_{2}>0
, where
\mathcal{A}
is
\sigma
-subalgebra of
\mathbb{P}
. Then, for any
t\in\mathbb{R}

\mathbb{P}(V>t|\mathcal{A})\leq\mathbb{P}(W>t-r_{1}|\mathcal{A})+r_{2}.

Proof of Lemma
G.1
.

Note that if
V>t
then either
W>t-r_{1}
or
|W-V|\geq r_{1}
.
Thus, for any
A\in\mathcal{A}
,
\mathbbm{1}\{V>t\}\mathbbm{1}_{A}\leq(\mathbbm{1}\{W>t-r_{1}\}+\mathbbm{1}\{|W-V|\geq r_{1}\})\mathbbm{1}_{A}
. The result follows by taking expectations, noting that
A\in\mathcal{A}
was arbitrary, and using the definition of conditional expectation.
∎

Definition G.2

(Lighter notation)
.

To streamline notation, we define the functions
Q
,
R
,
\Delta
,
L
, and
B
so that the following statements hold:

1.

There exists a Gaussian random element
Z
in
H
such that with probability at least
1-\eta

\left\lVert\sqrt{n}(\hat{f}-f_{\lambda})-Z\right\rVert\leq Q(n,\lambda,\eta).

2.

There exists a random element
Z^{\prime}
in
H
whose conditional distribution given
U
is almost surely Gaussian with covariance
\Sigma
, and with probability at least
1-\eta
,

\mathbb{P}\left[\vphantom{\sum}\left\lVert\mathfrak{B}-Z^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq R(n,\lambda,\eta)\middle|U\right]\geq 1-\eta.

3.

We define
\Delta
by
\Delta(n,\lambda,\eta)\coloneqq Q(n,\lambda,\eta)+R(n,\lambda,\eta)

4.

It holds with probability
1-\eta
that

\lVert Z\rVert\geq L(\lambda,\eta)
for some strictly increasing function
L
.

5.

\sqrt{n}\lVert f_{\lambda}-f_{0}\rVert\leq B(\lambda,n)
.

We abbreviate these quantities by
Q
,
R
,
\Delta
,
L
, and
B
, respectively.

Proposition G.3

(High probability events)
.

With probability
1-\eta
it simultaneously holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\lVert Z^{\prime}\rVert>t+\Delta|U)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(\lVert\mathfrak{B}\rVert>t|U)+\eta

(14)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\sqrt{n}\lVert\hat{f}-f_{0}\rVert>t+\Delta+B)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(\lVert\mathfrak{B}\rVert>t|U)+2\eta

(15)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\lVert\mathfrak{B}\rVert>t+\Delta+B|U)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(\sqrt{n}\lVert\hat{f}-f_{0}\rVert>t)+2\eta.

(16)

Proof.

The proof is entirely analogous to Theorem
G.6
, only we replace the use of Lemma
G.4
by Lemma
G.1
:

\mathbb{P}(|V-W|>r_{1}|\mathcal{A})\leq r_{2}\implies\mathbb{P}(V>t|\mathcal{A})\leq\mathbb{P}(W>t-r_{1}|\mathcal{A})+r_{2}.

We proceed in steps.

1.

First, recall that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\sqrt{n}(\hat{f}-f_{0})-Z\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert\sqrt{n}(f_{\lambda}-f_{0})\right\rVert+\left\lVert\sqrt{n}(\hat{f}-f_{\lambda})-Z\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq B+Q

with probability at least
1-\eta
, for some Gaussian random variable
Z
with covariance
\Sigma
.
By Lemma
G.1
with
\mathcal{A}
chosen to be the trivial
\sigma
-algebra,
V=\sqrt{n}\lVert\hat{f}-f_{0}\rVert
,
W=\lVert Z\rVert
,
r_{1}=Q+B
, and
r_{2}=\eta
we then have

\mathbb{P}\left[\sqrt{n}\lVert\hat{f}-f_{0}\rVert>t\right]\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\vphantom{\sum}\lVert Z\rVert>t-(Q+B)\right)+\eta.

Taking
t=s+Q+R+B
,

\mathbb{P}\left[\sqrt{n}\lVert\hat{f}-f_{0}\rVert>s+Q+R+B\right]\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\vphantom{\sum}\lVert Z\rVert>s+R\right)+\eta.

2.

Next, by Proposition
G.15

on an event
\mathcal{E}_{1}
with probability
1-\eta
,

\mathbb{P}\left[\vphantom{\sum}\left\lVert\mathfrak{B}-Z^{\prime}\right\rVert>R\middle|U\right]\leq\eta

for some
Z^{\prime}
whose conditional distribution is Gaussian with covariance
\Sigma
.
By Lemma
G.1
, with
\mathcal{A}=\sigma(U)
},
with
V=\|Z^{\prime}\|
,
W=\|\mathfrak{B}\|
,
r_{1}=R
, and
r_{2}=\eta
, it follows that on
\mathcal{E}_{1}
,

\mathbb{P}\left[\|Z^{\prime}\|>t\middle|U\right]\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\|\mathfrak{B}\|>t-R\middle|U\right)+\eta.

Taking
t=s+R
, on
\mathcal{E}_{1}
,

\mathbb{P}\left[\|Z^{\prime}\|>s+R\middle|U\right]\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\|\mathfrak{B}\|>s\middle|U\right)+\eta.

In light of the fact that
R\leq\Delta
, this immediately implies (
14
).

3.

Since the conditional distribution of
Z^{\prime}
given
U
is Gaussian with covariance
\Sigma
, as is the marginal distribution of
Z
, we can combine the previous two steps to deduce that on
\mathcal{E}_{1}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\sqrt{n}\lVert\hat{f}-f_{0}\rVert>s+Q+R+B\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\vphantom{\sum}\lVert Z\rVert>s+R\right)+\eta

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\vphantom{\sum}\|Z^{\prime}\|>s+R|U\right)+\eta

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\vphantom{\sum}\|\mathfrak{B}\|>s|U\right)+2\eta.

Noting that
\Delta=Q+R
gives (
15
).

4.

Reversing the argument with the roles of
V
and
W
interchanged in Lemma
G.1
yields (
16
). In particular, on
\mathcal{E}_{1}
,

\mathbb{P}\left[\|\mathfrak{B}\|>t\middle|U\right]\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\|Z^{\prime}\|>t-R\middle|U\right)+\eta.

Taking
t=s+Q+R+B
, then on
\mathcal{E}_{1}
,

\mathbb{P}\left[\|\mathfrak{B}\|>s+Q+R+B\middle|U\right]\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\|Z^{\prime}\|>s+Q+B\middle|U\right)+\eta.

Moreover,

\mathbb{P}\left[\vphantom{\sum}\lVert Z\rVert>t\right]\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\sqrt{n}\lVert\hat{f}-f_{0}\rVert>t-(Q+B)\right)+\eta.

Taking
t=s+Q+B
,

\mathbb{P}\left[\vphantom{\sum}\lVert Z\rVert>s+Q+B\right]\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\sqrt{n}\lVert\hat{f}-f_{0}\rVert>s\right)+\eta.

Therefore on
\mathcal{E}_{1}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\|\mathfrak{B}\|>s+Q+R+B|U\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\vphantom{\sum}\lVert Z^{\prime}\rVert>s+Q+B|U\right)+\eta

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\vphantom{\sum}\|Z\|>s+Q+B\right)+\eta

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\sqrt{n}\lVert\hat{f}-f_{0}\rVert>s\right)+2\eta.

∎

Proof of Theorem
6.2
.

Let
\mathcal{E}_{1}
denote the event in Proposition
G.3
. We proceed in steps.

1.

Let us first work conditioned upon this
\mathcal{E}_{1}
. By (
14
) in Proposition
G.3
, and since the law of
Z
is the same as the conditional law of
Z^{\prime}
given
U
, it holds that

\mathbb{P}(\lVert Z\rVert>\hat{t}_{\alpha}+\Delta)=\mathbb{P}(\lVert Z^{\prime}\rVert>\hat{t}_{\alpha}+\Delta|U)\leq\alpha+\eta.

Meanwhile, by Lemma
G.13
and the fact that
L
is strictly increasing in its latter argument, we have that
L(\lambda,1-\alpha-2\eta)<L(\lambda,1-\alpha-\eta)
and hence

\mathbb{P}\{\left\lVert Z\right\rVert>L(\lambda,1-\alpha-2\eta)\}\geq\mathbb{P}\{\left\lVert Z\right\rVert\geq L(\lambda,1-\alpha-\eta)\}\geq\alpha+\eta.

Thus, putting
\tilde{L}\coloneqq L(\lambda,1-\alpha-2\eta)
, we can conclude that

\mathbb{P}\{\left\lVert Z\right\rVert>\tilde{L}\}\geq\mathbb{P}(\lVert Z\rVert>\hat{t}_{\alpha}+\Delta)

and therefore
\hat{t}_{\alpha}+\Delta\geq\tilde{L}\iff\hat{t}_{\alpha}\geq\tilde{L}-\Delta
.

2.

Consider any
\delta
that satisfies

\frac{1}{2}\geq\delta\geq\frac{\Delta+B(n,\lambda)}{\tilde{L}-\Delta}.

Then

\delta\hat{t}_{\alpha}\geq\frac{\Delta+B(n,\lambda)}{\tilde{L}-\Delta}\tilde{L}-\Delta=\Delta+B(n,\lambda).

It follows that
(1+\delta)\hat{t}_{\alpha}\geq\hat{t}_{\alpha}+B+\Delta
, so by (
15
) in Proposition
G.3
, we have

\mathbb{P}\{\sqrt{n}\lVert\hat{f}-f_{0}\rVert>(1+\delta)\hat{t}_{\alpha}\}\leq\alpha+2\eta.

Another implication is that
(1-\delta)\hat{t}_{\alpha}\leq\hat{t}_{\alpha}-B-\Delta
, so by (
16
) in Proposition
G.3
we have that

\mathbb{P}\{\sqrt{n}\lVert\hat{f}-f_{0}\rVert>(1-\delta)\hat{t}_{\alpha}\}\geq\alpha-2\eta.

3.

Finally, note that on the complementary event
\mathcal{E}_{1}^{c}
,
\mathbb{P}\{\sqrt{n}\lVert\hat{f}-f_{0}\rVert>-\}
must take values between
0
and
1
.

One implication is that, unconditionally, we must have

\mathbb{P}\{\sqrt{n}\lVert\hat{f}-f_{0}\rVert>(1+\delta)\hat{t}_{\alpha}\}\leq\alpha+3\eta\iff\mathbb{P}\{\sqrt{n}\lVert\hat{f}-f_{0}\rVert\leq(1+\delta)\hat{t}_{\alpha}\}>1-\alpha-3\eta.

Recall that
\hat{S}_{n}
is
\tau
-honest
at level
\alpha
if

\mathbb{P}(f_{0}\in\hat{S}_{n})\geq 1-\alpha-\tau.

Therefore, unconditionally, the confidence set given by
\hat{S}_{\alpha}

is
3\eta
honest.

Another implication is that, unconditionally,

\mathbb{P}\{\sqrt{n}\lVert\hat{f}-f_{0}\rVert>(1-\delta)\hat{t}_{\alpha}\}\geq\alpha-2\eta\iff\mathbb{P}\{\sqrt{n}\lVert\hat{f}-f_{0}\rVert\leq(1-\delta)\hat{t}_{\alpha}\}<1-\alpha+2\eta.

Recall that
\hat{S}_{n}
is
(\delta^{\prime},\tau)
-sharp
at level
\alpha
if

\mathbb{P}\{f_{0}\in\delta^{\prime}\hat{f}+(1-\delta^{\prime})\hat{S}_{n}\}\leq 1-\alpha+\tau.

We finally show that, unconditionally, the confidence set given by
\hat{S}_{\alpha}

is
(2\delta,2\eta)
-sharp. It suffices to show that

\sqrt{n}\lVert\hat{f}-f_{0}\rVert\leq(1-\delta)\hat{t}_{\alpha}\implies f_{0}\in 2\delta\hat{f}+(1-2\delta)\hat{S}_{n}.

The latter expression may be rewritten as

\|\hat{f}-f_{0}\|\leq(1-2\delta)\|h\|\leq(1-2\delta)(1+\delta)\hat{t}_{\alpha}n^{-1/2}\leq(1-\delta)\hat{t}_{\alpha}n^{-1/2}

by the definition of
\hat{S}_{n}
and the fact that
\delta\leq 1/2
implies
(1-2\delta)(1+\delta)\leq(1-\delta)
.

∎

G.2
Anti-concentration approach

Having coupled the distributions of the ridge error process
\sqrt{n}(\hat{f}-f_{\lambda})
and that of the feasible bootstrap process
\mathfrak{B}
with respect to the norm in
H
, we obtain valid inference for any functional
F:H\mapsto\mathbb{R}
provided that uniform continuity and anti-concentration conditions are satisfied.

Lemma G.4

(cf.
Chernozhukov et al., 2016
)
.

Let
V,W
be real-valued random variables such that
\mathbb{P}(|V-W|>r_{1}|\mathcal{A})\leq r_{2}
for some constants
r_{1},r_{2}>0
where
\mathcal{A}
is a
\sigma
-algebra comprised of Borel sets. Then we have

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\vphantom{\sum}\mathbb{P}(V\leq t|\mathcal{A})-\mathbb{P}(W\leq t|\mathcal{A})\right|\leq\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{P}\left(\vphantom{\sum}|W-t|\leq r_{1}\middle|\mathcal{A}\right)+r_{2}.

Proof.

We proceed in steps.

1.

To begin, we show that for fixed
(V,W,t,z)
, where
z>0
,

\mathbbm{1}\{V\leq t\}-\mathbbm{1}\{W\leq t\}\leq\mathbbm{1}\{|W-t|\leq z\}\vee\mathbbm{1}\{|V-W|>z\}.

If the left-hand side is
1
, then
V\leq t<W
, so
|W-t|<|W-V|
. Thus, it is not possible
|W-t|>z\geq|W-V|
, so the right-hand side is also
1
. Otherwise the left hand side is at most
0
and the right hand side is at least
0
.

2.

Taking
z=r_{1}
, we write

\displaystyle\mathbbm{1}\{V\leq t\}-\mathbbm{1}\{W\leq t\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbbm{1}\{|W-t|\leq r_{1}\}\vee\mathbbm{1}\{|V-W|>r_{1}\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbbm{1}\{|W-t|\leq r_{1}\}+\mathbbm{1}\{|V-W|>r_{1}\}.

Thus, for any
A\in\mathcal{A}
, multiplication by
\mathbbm{1}_{A}
yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{A}\mathbbm{1}\{V\leq t\}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{A}\mathbbm{1}\{W\leq t\}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{A}\mathbbm{1}\{|W-t|\leq r_{1}\}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbbm{1}_{A}\mathbbm{1}\{|V-W|>r_{1}\}\right]

so that by the definition of conditional expectation

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(V\leq t|\mathcal{A})-\mathbb{P}(W\leq t|\mathcal{A})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(|W-t|\leq r_{1}|\mathcal{A})+\mathbb{P}(|V-W|>r_{1}|\mathcal{A})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(|W-t|\leq r_{1}|\mathcal{A})+r_{2}.

3.

Now, note that the same bound holds with the roles of
V
and
W
interchanged, so that we may replace the left-hand side by its absolute value. Finally, we take the supremum over
t\in\mathbb{R}
.

∎

Definition G.5

(Heavier notation)
.

In what follows, define

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathrm{bd}}

\displaystyle:=\psi\left[C^{\prime}M\log(16/\eta)^{2}\left\{Q_{\mathrm{bd}}(T,n,\lambda)+R_{\mathrm{bd}}(T,n,\lambda)+\frac{\kappa\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}{\sqrt{n}}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathrm{sg}}

\displaystyle:=\psi\left[C^{\prime}\bar{M}^{4}b^{3}(16/\eta)^{1/\log(n)}\left\{Q_{\mathrm{sg}}(T,n,\lambda)+R_{\mathrm{sg}}(T,n,\lambda)+\frac{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}{\sqrt{n}}\right\}\right]

where
C^{\prime}
is a sufficiently large universal constant and

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} Q_{\mathrm{bd}}(n,\lambda)&=\frac{1}{\lambda}\inf_{m\geq 1}\left\{\sigma(m)+\frac{m^{2}\log(m^{2})}{\sqrt{n}}\right\},\\
R_{\mathrm{bd}}(n,\lambda)&=\inf_{m\geq 1}\left[\left\{\frac{m\,\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}{\lambda^{2}n}+\frac{m}{\lambda^{4}n^{2}}\right\}^{\frac{1}{4}}+\frac{\sigma(m)}{\lambda}\right],\\
\end{aligned}\quad\begin{aligned} Q_{\mathrm{sg}}(n,\lambda)&=\inf_{m\geq 1}\left\{\frac{\sigma(m)}{\lambda}+\frac{m^{\frac{3}{2}}\log(n)^{2}}{\sqrt{n\lambda}}\right\},\\
R_{\mathrm{sg}}(n,\lambda)&=\inf_{m\geq 1}\left[\left\{\frac{m\,\mathfrak{n}^{2}(\lambda)}{n}\right\}^{\frac{1}{4}}+\frac{\sigma(m)}{\lambda}\right].\\
\end{aligned}

Theorem G.6

(Inference)
.

Suppose
F
is uniformly continuous.

1.

If in addition the conditions of Propositions
G.14
and
G.15
hold, set
\Delta=\Delta_{\mathrm{bd}}
.

2.

If in addition the conditions of Proposition
G.16
and
G.17
hold, set
\Delta=\Delta_{\mathrm{sg}}
.

Then with probability
1-\eta
:

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbb{P}\left[F\Big\{\sqrt{n}(\hat{f}-f_{\lambda})\Big\}\leq t\right]-\mathbb{P}\left\{\vphantom{\sum}F(\mathfrak{B})\leq t\middle|U\right\}\right|\leq 2\left[\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{P}\left\{\vphantom{\sum}|F(\mathfrak{B})-t|\leq 2\Delta\middle|U\right\}+\eta\right].

If in addition Assumption
6.5
holds, then on this same event we have

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbb{P}\left[F\Big\{\sqrt{n}(\hat{f}-f_{\lambda})\Big\}\leq t\right]-\mathbb{P}\left\{\vphantom{\sum}F(\mathfrak{B})\leq t\middle|U\right\}\right|\leq 2(\zeta\Delta+\eta).

Proof.

We proceed in steps, focusing on the case where
\Delta=\Delta_{\mathrm{bd}}
.

1.

First, recall that by Proposition
G.14
,

\left\lVert\sqrt{n}(\hat{f}-f_{\lambda})-Z\right\rVert\lesssim Q_{\mathrm{bd}}(T,n,\lambda)M\log(12/\eta)+\frac{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}{\sqrt{n}}M\log(8/\eta)^{2}

with probability at least
1-\eta
, for some Gaussian random variable
Z
with covariance
\Sigma
.
Using uniform continuity of the functional
F
, it follows that on this same event,

|F\{\sqrt{n}(\hat{f}-f_{\lambda})\}-F(Z)|\leq\psi\left[C^{\prime}M\log(12/\eta)^{2}\left\{Q_{\mathrm{bd}}(T,n,\lambda)+\frac{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}{\sqrt{n}}\right\}\right]\leq\Delta.

By Lemma
G.4
(with
\mathcal{A}
chosen to be the trivial
\sigma
-algebra) with
V=F\{\sqrt{n}(\hat{f}-f_{\lambda})\}
,
W=F(Z)
,
r_{1}=\Delta
, and
r_{2}=\eta
we then have

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbb{P}\left[F\Big\{\sqrt{n}(\hat{f}-f_{\lambda})\Big\}\leq t\right]-\mathbb{P}\left\{\vphantom{\sum}F(Z)\leq t\right\}\right|\leq\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{P}\left\{\vphantom{\sum}|F(Z)-t|\leq\Delta\middle|\mathcal{A}\right\}+\eta.

(17)

The same argument holds for
\Delta=\Delta_{\mathrm{sg}}
, instead appealing to Proposition
G.16
.

2.

Next, by Proposition
G.15

with probability
1-\eta
,

\mathbb{P}\left[\left\lVert\mathfrak{B}-Z^{\prime}\right\rVert\lesssim M\log(16/\eta)^{2}\left\{R_{\mathrm{bd}}(T,n,\lambda)+\frac{\kappa\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}{\sqrt{n}}\right\}\middle|U\right]\geq 1-\eta

for some
Z^{\prime}
whose conditional distribution is Gaussian with covariance
\Sigma
.
Again, by uniform continuity of
F
, we have on this event that

\mathbb{P}\left\{\vphantom{\sum}|F(\mathfrak{B})-F(Z^{\prime})|>\Delta\middle|U\right\}\leq\eta

(18)

where
C^{\prime}
in the definition of
\Delta
may have increased to absorb the universal constant in the preceding display.

By Lemma
G.4
, {with
\mathcal{A}=\sigma(U)
} with
V=F(\mathfrak{B})
,
W=F(Z^{\prime})
,
r_{1}=\Delta
, and
r_{2}=\eta
, it follows that with probability at least
1-\eta
,

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\bigg|\mathbb{P}\left\{F(\mathfrak{B})\leq t\middle|U\right\}-\mathbb{P}\left\{F(Z^{\prime})\leq t\middle|U\right\}\bigg|\leq\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{P}\left\{\vphantom{\sum}|F(Z^{\prime})-t|\leq\Delta\middle|U\right\}+\eta.

(19)

The same argument holds for
\Delta=\Delta_{\mathrm{sg}}
, instead appealing to Proposition
G.17
.

3.

To recover the first statement, we provide bounds on (
17
) and (
19
). We begin by showing that, for any
\delta>0
,

\mathcal{E}_{1}=\{|F(Z^{\prime})-t|\leq\delta\mid U\}\subset\{|F(\mathfrak{B})-t|\leq 2\delta\mid U\}\cup\{|F(\mathfrak{B})-F(Z^{\prime})|>\delta\mid U\}=\mathcal{E}_{2}.

We prove this result by contradiction; we argue that
\mathcal{E}_{2}^{c}\subset\mathcal{E}_{1}^{c}
. To begin, write

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{2}^{c}

\displaystyle=\{|F(\mathfrak{B})-t|\leq 2\delta\mid U\}^{c}\cap\{|F(\mathfrak{B})-F(Z^{\prime})|>\delta\mid U\}^{c}

\displaystyle=\{|F(\mathfrak{B})-t|>2\delta\mid U\}\cap\{|F(\mathfrak{B})-F(Z^{\prime})|\leq\delta\mid U\}.

Under
\mathcal{E}_{2}^{c}
, conditional on
U
,

2\delta<|F(\mathfrak{B})-t|\leq|F(\mathfrak{B})-F(Z^{\prime})|+|F(Z^{\prime})-t|\leq\delta+|F(Z^{\prime})-t|

which implies
\delta<|F(Z^{\prime})-t|
, i.e.
\mathcal{E}_{1}^{c}
. Therefore

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\vphantom{\sum}|F(Z)-t|\leq\delta\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left\{\vphantom{\sum}|F(Z^{\prime})-t|\leq\delta\middle|U\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{1})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{2})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{\vphantom{\sum}|F(\mathfrak{B})-t|\leq 2\delta\middle|U\right\}+\mathbb{P}\left\{\vphantom{\sum}|F(Z^{\prime})-F(\mathfrak{B})|>\delta\middle|U\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{\vphantom{\sum}|F(\mathfrak{B})-t|\leq 2\delta\middle|U\right\}+\eta

where the last inequality hold with probability
1-\eta
conditional on our chosen event and (
18
). Taking the supremum over
t\in\mathbb{R}
and setting
\delta=\Delta
yields

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{P}\left\{\vphantom{\sum}|F(Z)-t|\leq\Delta\right\}=\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{P}\left\{\vphantom{\sum}|F(Z^{\prime})-t|\leq\Delta\middle|U\right\}\leq\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{P}\left\{\vphantom{\sum}|F(\mathfrak{B})-t|\leq 2\Delta\middle|U\right\}+\eta.

Thus, by the triangle inequality for the sup-norm, with probability
1-\eta
,

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbb{P}\left[F\Big\{\sqrt{n}(\hat{f}-f_{\lambda})\Big\}\leq t\right]-\mathbb{P}\left\{\vphantom{\sum}F(\mathfrak{B})\leq t\middle|U\right\}\right|\leq 2\left[\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{P}\left\{\vphantom{\sum}|F(\mathfrak{B})-t|\leq 2\Delta\middle|U\right\}+\eta\right],

proving the first statement.

4.

To recover the second statement, we use Assumption
6.5
to provide alternative bounds for the right hand side of (
17
) and (
19
).

In particular for (
17
), recall that there exists
\zeta>0
, potentially depending on
n
and
\lambda
, such that

\zeta\coloneqq\sup_{\delta>0}\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left[\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{P}\left\{\vphantom{\sum}|F(Z)-t|\leq\delta\middle|\mathcal{A}\right\}\right]<\infty.

Hence for
\delta=\Delta

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left[\frac{1}{\Delta}\mathbb{P}\left\{\vphantom{\sum}|F(Z)-t|\leq\Delta\middle|\mathcal{A}\right\}\right]<\zeta.

In summary

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbb{P}\left[F\Big\{\sqrt{n}(\hat{f}-f_{\lambda})\Big\}\leq t\right]-\mathbb{P}\left\{\vphantom{\sum}F(Z)\leq t\right\}\right|\leq\zeta\Delta+\eta.

Since
Z^{\prime}
is conditionally Gaussian with covariance
\Sigma
, we appeal to Assumption
6.5
to further bound the right hand side of (
19
) as well. In particular, recall that there exists
\zeta>0
, potentially depending on
n
and
\lambda
, such that

\zeta\coloneqq\sup_{\delta>0}\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left[\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{P}\left\{\vphantom{\sum}|F(Z^{\prime})-t|\leq\delta\middle|U\right\}\right]<\infty.

Hence for
\delta=\Delta

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left[\frac{1}{\Delta}\mathbb{P}\left\{\vphantom{\sum}|F(Z^{\prime})-t|\leq\Delta\middle|\mathcal{A}\right\}\right]<\zeta.

We conclude that, with probability
1-\eta
,

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\bigg|\mathbb{P}\left\{F(\mathfrak{B})\leq t\middle|U\right\}-\mathbb{P}\left\{F(Z^{\prime})\leq t\middle|U\right\}\bigg|\leq\zeta\Delta+\eta.

Thus, by the triangle inequality for the sup-norm, since
Z\sim Z^{\prime}|U
, it holds with probability
1-\eta
that

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbb{P}\left[F\Big\{\sqrt{n}(\hat{f}-f_{\lambda})\Big\}\leq t\right]-\mathbb{P}\left\{\vphantom{\sum}F(\mathfrak{B})\leq t\middle|U\right\}\right|\leq 2(\zeta\Delta+\eta),

proving the second statement.

∎

G.3
Bounding key terms

G.3.1
Covariance operator

To this end, set

U_{i}=T_{\lambda}^{-1}[(k_{X_{i}}\otimes k_{X_{i}}^{*}-T)(f_{0}-f_{\lambda})+\varepsilon_{i}k_{X_{i}}],\quad\Sigma=\mathbb{E}[U_{i}\otimes U_{i}^{*}].

In order to apply our coupling results, we will need to make the following estimates on the covariance of
U_{i}
.

Lemma G.7

(Upper bounding the covariance)
.

We have

0\preceq\Sigma\preceq(\kappa^{2}\left\lVert f_{0}\right\rVert^{2}+\bar{\sigma}^{2})T_{\lambda}^{-2}T.

Proof.

We begin by using bi-linearity of the tensor product to expand

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[U_{i}\otimes U_{i}^{*}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{\vphantom{\sum}T_{\lambda}^{-1}(k_{X_{i}}\otimes k_{X_{i}}^{*}-T)(f_{0}-f_{\lambda})\right\}\otimes\left\{\vphantom{\sum}T_{\lambda}^{-1}(k_{X_{i}}\otimes k_{X_{i}}^{*}-T)(f_{0}-f_{\lambda})\right\}^{*}\right]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{\vphantom{\sum}T_{\lambda}^{-1}(k_{X_{i}}\otimes k_{X_{i}}^{*}-T)(f_{0}-f_{\lambda})\right\}\otimes\left\{\vphantom{\sum}\varepsilon_{i}T_{\lambda}^{-1}k_{X_{i}}\right\}^{*}\right]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{\vphantom{\sum}\varepsilon_{i}T_{\lambda}^{-1}k_{X_{i}}\right\}\otimes\left\{\vphantom{\sum}T_{\lambda}^{-1}(k_{X_{i}}\otimes k_{X_{i}}^{*}-T)(f_{0}-f_{\lambda})\right\}^{*}\right]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{\vphantom{\sum}\varepsilon_{i}T_{\lambda}^{-1}k_{X_{i}}\right\}\otimes\left\{\vphantom{\sum}\varepsilon_{i}T_{\lambda}^{-1}k_{X_{i}}\right\}^{*}\right].

Since
\mathbb{E}[\epsilon_{i}|X_{i}]=0
, second and third terms are zero.
For the first term, since

0\preceq\mathbb{E}[(f-\mathbb{E}f)\otimes(f-\mathbb{E}f)^{*}]=\mathbb{E}[f\otimes f^{*}]-\left(\mathbb{E}[f]\otimes\mathbb{E}[f]\right)^{*}\preceq\mathbb{E}[f\otimes f^{*}],

we have

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\preceq\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{\vphantom{\sum}T_{\lambda}^{-1}(k_{X_{i}}\otimes k_{X_{i}}^{*}-T)(f_{0}-f_{\lambda})\right\}\otimes\left\{\vphantom{\sum}T_{\lambda}^{-1}(k_{X_{i}}\otimes k_{X_{i}}^{*}-T)(f_{0}-f_{\lambda})\right\}^{*}\right]

\displaystyle\preceq\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{\vphantom{\sum}T_{\lambda}^{-1}(k_{X_{i}}\otimes k_{X_{i}}^{*})(f_{0}-f_{\lambda})\right\}\otimes\left\{\vphantom{\sum}T_{\lambda}^{-1}(k_{X_{i}}\otimes k_{X_{i}}^{*})(f_{0}-f_{\lambda})\right\}^{*}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[(T_{\lambda}^{-1}k_{X_{i}})\otimes\left(T_{\lambda}^{-1}k_{X_{i}}\right)^{*}\{f_{0}(X_{i})-f_{\lambda}(X_{i})\}^{2}\right].

Since the fourth term is also clearly positive definite, we have

0\preceq\Sigma\preceq\mathbb{E}\left(\left\{\vphantom{\sum}(T_{\lambda}^{-1}k_{X_{i}})\otimes(T_{\lambda}^{-1}k_{X_{i}})^{*}\right\}\left[\vphantom{\sum}\{f_{0}(X_{i})-f_{\lambda}(X_{i})\}^{2}+\varepsilon_{i}^{2}\right]\right).

Finally, it suffices to use the almost sure bounds given by
|\varepsilon_{i}|\leq\bar{\sigma}
and

\left|f_{0}(X_{i})-f_{\lambda}(X_{i})\right|=\left|\left\langle f_{0}-f_{\lambda},\,k_{X_{i}}\right\rangle\right|=\left|\left\langle(I-T_{\lambda}^{-1}T)f_{0},\,k_{X_{i}}\right\rangle\right|\leq\kappa\left\lVert f_{0}\right\rVert,

the latter of which follows by Cauchy-Schwartz and the fact that
\left\lVert I-T_{\lambda}^{-1}T\right\rVert_{op}\leq 1
since it is a difference of two positive definite operators of norm at most
1
. Lastly, we have

\mathbb{E}\left\{(T_{\lambda}^{-1}k_{X_{i}})\otimes\left(T_{\lambda}^{-1}k_{X_{i}}\right)^{*}\right\}=\mathbb{E}\{T_{\lambda}^{-1}(k_{X_{i}}\otimes k_{X_{i}}^{*})T_{\lambda}^{-1}\}=T_{\lambda}^{-1}TT_{\lambda}^{-1},

since
\mathbb{E}[k_{X_{i}}\otimes k_{X_{i}}^{*}]=T
. To arrive at the result, note that
T
commutes with
T_{\lambda}^{-1}
.
∎

Lemma G.8

(Lower bounding the covariance)
.

If
\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_{i}^{2}|X_{i}]\geq\underline{\sigma}^{2}
then
\Sigma\succeq\underline{\sigma}^{2}T_{\lambda}^{-2}T
.

Proof.

As argued in the proof of Lemma
G.7

\displaystyle\Sigma

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{1}+\Sigma_{2}

\displaystyle\Sigma_{1}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{\vphantom{\sum}T_{\lambda}^{-1}(k_{X_{i}}\otimes k_{X_{i}}^{*}-T)(f_{0}-f_{\lambda})\right\}\otimes\left\{\vphantom{\sum}T_{\lambda}^{-1}(k_{X_{i}}\otimes k_{X_{i}}^{*}-T)(f_{0}-f_{\lambda})\right\}^{*}\right]\succeq 0

\displaystyle\Sigma_{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{\vphantom{\sum}\varepsilon_{i}T_{\lambda}^{-1}k_{X_{i}}\right\}\otimes\left\{\vphantom{\sum}\varepsilon_{i}T_{\lambda}^{-1}k_{X_{i}}\right\}^{*}\right]\succeq 0.

Hence

\displaystyle\Sigma

\displaystyle\succeq\Sigma_{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{\vphantom{\sum}\varepsilon_{i}T_{\lambda}^{-1}k_{X_{i}}\right\}\otimes\left\{\vphantom{\sum}\varepsilon_{i}T_{\lambda}^{-1}k_{X_{i}}\right\}^{*}\right]

\displaystyle\succeq\underline{\sigma}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{\vphantom{\sum}T_{\lambda}^{-1}k_{X_{i}}\right\}\otimes\left\{\vphantom{\sum}T_{\lambda}^{-1}k_{X_{i}}\right\}^{*}\right]

\displaystyle=\underline{\sigma}^{2}T_{\lambda}^{-1}TT_{\lambda}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\underline{\sigma}^{2}T_{\lambda}^{-2}T.

∎

Lemma G.9

(Spectral ordering)
.

If
A\preceq B
for some trace-class, self-adjoint operators
A
and
B
, then

\sigma^{2}(A,m)\leq\sigma^{2}(B,m).

In particular, taking
m=0
, we recover
\tr(A)\leq\tr(B)
.

Proof.

Recall the definition

\sigma^{2}(A,m)=\sum_{s=m+1}^{\infty}\nu_{s}(A)=\sum_{s=m+1}^{\infty}\left\langle e_{s}(A),\,Ae_{s}(A)\right\rangle,

for a self-adjoint operator
A
with finite trace, where
\{\nu_{1}(A),\nu_{2}(A),\ldots\}
are the eigenvalues of
A
listed in decreasing order and
\{e_{1}(A),e_{2}(A),\ldots\}
are the corresponding eigenvectors. Now, let
(f_{1},f_{2},\ldots)
denote any other orthonormal basis of
H
. It follows by the variational representation of the top
m
eigenvectors that

\sum_{s=1}^{m}\nu_{s}(A)\geq\sum_{s=1}^{m}\left\langle f_{s},\,Af_{s}\right\rangle.

Thus, if
A\preceq B
for some trace-class, self-adjoint operator
B
, and taking
f_{s}=e_{s}(B)
,

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}(A,m)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=m+1}^{\infty}\nu_{s}(A)

\displaystyle=\tr(A)-\sum_{s=1}^{m}\nu_{s}(A)

\displaystyle\leq\tr(A)-\sum_{s=1}^{m}\left\langle e_{s}(B),\,Ae_{s}(B)\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=m+1}^{\infty}\left\langle e_{s}(B),\,Ae_{s}(B)\right\rangle,

since the trace is independent of the choice of orthonormal basis. Since
A\preceq B
, this is

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s=m+1}^{\infty}\left\langle e_{s}(B),\,Be_{s}(B)\right\rangle=\sigma^{2}(B,m).

∎

Lemma G.10

(Local width bounds)
.

In our setting we have the bounds

\sigma(\Sigma,m)\leq\left(\frac{\kappa\left\lVert f_{0}\right\rVert+\bar{\sigma}}{\lambda}\right)\sigma(T,m),\quad\sigma(\Sigma,0)\leq(\kappa\left\lVert f_{0}\right\rVert+\bar{\sigma})\sqrt{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}.

Proof.

We appeal to Lemmas
G.7
and
G.9
. Recall the bound on
\Sigma=\mathbb{E}[U_{i}\otimes U_{i}^{*}]
is

0\preceq\Sigma\preceq(\kappa\left\lVert f_{0}\right\rVert+\bar{\sigma})^{2}T_{\lambda}^{-2}T\preceq\left(\frac{\kappa\left\lVert f_{0}\right\rVert+\bar{\sigma}}{\lambda}\right)^{2}T.

Then, since
\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)\coloneqq\tr(T_{\lambda}^{-2}T)
, we have

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}(\Sigma,0)

\displaystyle=\tr(\Sigma)\leq(\kappa\left\lVert f_{0}\right\rVert+\bar{\sigma})^{2}\tr(T_{\lambda}^{-2}T)=(\kappa\left\lVert f_{0}\right\rVert+\bar{\sigma})^{2}\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)

and

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}(\Sigma,m)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{\kappa\left\lVert f_{0}\right\rVert+\bar{\sigma}}{\lambda}\right)^{2}\sigma^{2}(T,m).

Taking square roots recovers the second and third stated inequalities.
∎

G.3.2
Summands

Lemma G.11

(Bounded summands)
.

We may appeal to the bounded summand results using

\left\lVert U_{i}\right\rVert\leq\left(\frac{\kappa^{2}\left\lVert f_{0}\right\rVert+\kappa\bar{\sigma}}{\lambda}\right).

Proof.

Write

\displaystyle\left\lVert U_{i}\right\rVert

\displaystyle=\left\lVert T_{\lambda}^{-1}[(k_{X_{i}}\otimes k_{X_{i}}^{*}-T)(f_{0}-f_{\lambda})+\varepsilon_{i}k_{X_{i}}]\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert T_{\lambda}^{-1}\right\rVert_{op}\left\{\vphantom{\sum}\left\lVert k_{X_{i}}\otimes k_{X_{i}}^{*}-T\right\rVert_{op}\left\lVert f_{0}-f_{\lambda}\right\rVert+\left\lVert\varepsilon_{i}k_{X_{i}}\right\rVert\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\lambda}\left\{\vphantom{\sum}\left\lVert k_{X_{i}}\otimes k_{X_{i}}^{*}-T\right\rVert_{op}\left\lVert f_{0}-f_{\lambda}\right\rVert+\bar{\sigma}\kappa\right\}.

Note that
k_{X_{i}}\otimes k_{X_{i}}^{*}-T
is a difference of two positive definite operators, so
\left\lVert k_{X_{i}}\otimes k_{X_{i}}^{*}-T\right\rVert_{op}\leq\left\lVert k_{X_{i}}\otimes k_{X_{i}}^{*}\right\rVert_{op}\vee\left\lVert T\right\rVert_{op}
. Further,
\left\lVert T\right\rVert_{op}=\left\lVert\mathbb{E}[k_{X_{i}}\otimes k_{X_{i}}^{*}]\right\rVert_{op}\leq\mathbb{E}\left\lVert k_{X_{i}}\otimes k_{X_{i}}^{*}\right\rVert_{op}
by Jensen’s inequality and
\left\lVert k_{X_{i}}\otimes k_{X_{i}}^{*}\right\rVert_{op}\leq\kappa^{2}
almost surely, so the above is

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\lambda}\left\{\vphantom{\sum}\kappa^{2}\left\lVert f_{0}-f_{\lambda}\right\rVert+\bar{\sigma}\kappa\right\}.

Finally,
f_{0}-f_{\lambda}=(I-T_{\lambda}^{-1}T)f_{0}
and
(I-T_{\lambda}^{-1}T)
is a difference of two positive definite operators of norm at most
1
, so the above is

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\lambda}\left\{\vphantom{\sum}\kappa^{2}\left\lVert I-T_{\lambda}^{-1}T\right\rVert_{op}\left\lVert f_{0}\right\rVert+\bar{\sigma}\kappa\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\lambda}\left\{\vphantom{\sum}\kappa^{2}\left\lVert f_{0}-f_{\lambda}\right\rVert+\bar{\sigma}\kappa\right\}.

∎

Lemma G.12

(Sub-Gaussian summands)
.

Suppose
k_{X_{i}}
is sub-Gaussian with parameter
b
and
\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_{i}^{2}|X_{i}]\geq\underline{\sigma}^{2}
. Then
U_{i}
is sub-Gaussian with parameter
\frac{(\kappa\left\lVert f_{0}\right\rVert+\bar{\sigma})}{\underline{\sigma}}b
.

Proof.

We proceed in steps.

1.

Equivalent expressions. By hypothesis, for all
t\in H
,
k_{X_{i}}
satisfies

\mathbb{P}\left(\left\langle t,\,k_{X_{i}}\right\rangle>ub\left(\mathbb{E}\left\langle t,\,k_{X_{i}}\right\rangle^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\leq 2e^{-u^{2}}.

As argued in Appendix
J
, this definition implies
\left\langle t,\,k_{X_{i}}\right\rangle
is sub-Gaussian with
\left\lVert\left\langle k_{X_{i}},\,t\right\rangle\right\rVert_{\psi_{2}}\leq Cb\left\langle t,\,Tt\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}
. To see why, notice that in our sub-Gaussian assumption,

b\left(\mathbb{E}\left\langle t,\,k_{X_{i}}\right\rangle^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}=b\left\langle t,\,Tt\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Therefore we are effectively assuming

\left\|\frac{\left\langle t,\,k_{X_{i}}\right\rangle}{b\left\langle t,\,Tt\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq C\iff\left\|\left\langle t,\,k_{X_{i}}\right\rangle\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq Cb\left\langle t,\,Tt\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}.

2.

Comparison. Recall that
U_{i}=\{f_{\lambda}(X_{i})-f_{0}(X_{i})+\varepsilon_{i}\}T_{\lambda}^{-1}k_{X_{i}}.
Thus, we have

\displaystyle|\left\langle U_{i},\,t\right\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq|f_{\lambda}(X_{i})-f_{0}(X_{i})+\varepsilon_{i}|\cdot|\left\langle T_{\lambda}^{-1}k_{X_{i}},\,t\right\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq(\kappa\left\lVert f_{0}\right\rVert+\bar{\sigma})|\left\langle T_{\lambda}^{-1}k_{X_{i}},\,t\right\rangle|

\displaystyle=(\kappa\left\lVert f_{0}\right\rVert+\bar{\sigma})|\left\langle k_{X_{i}},\,T_{\lambda}^{-1}t\right\rangle|.

Hence for any
u\geq(\kappa\left\lVert f_{0}\right\rVert+\bar{\sigma})\left\lVert\left\langle k_{X_{i}},\,T_{\lambda}^{-1}t\right\rangle\right\rVert_{\psi_{2}}
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\{\exp(\left\langle U_{i},\,t\right\rangle^{2}/u^{2})\}\leq\mathbb{E}\left\{\exp\left(\left\langle k_{X_{i}},\,T_{\lambda}^{-1}t\right\rangle^{2}/\left\lVert\left\langle k_{X_{i}},\,T_{\lambda}^{-1}t\right\rangle\right\rVert^{2}_{\psi_{2}}\right)\right\}\leq 2,

so we conclude that
\left\lVert\left\langle U_{i},\,t\right\rangle\right\rVert_{\psi_{2}}\leq(\kappa\left\lVert f_{0}\right\rVert+\bar{\sigma})\left\lVert\left\langle k_{X_{i}},\,T_{\lambda}^{-1}t\right\rangle\right\rVert_{\psi_{2}}
by definition of
\psi_{2}
norm as the smallest
u
such that the above holds.

3.

Lower bound. In summary, we have shown

\displaystyle\left\lVert\left\langle U_{i},\,t\right\rangle\right\rVert_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq(\kappa\left\lVert f_{0}\right\rVert+\bar{\sigma})\left\lVert\left\langle k_{X_{i}},\,T_{\lambda}^{-1}t\right\rangle\right\rVert_{\psi_{2}}\leq C(\kappa\left\lVert f_{0}\right\rVert+\bar{\sigma})b\left\langle t,\,T_{\lambda}^{-1}TT_{\lambda}^{-1}t\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}.

We wish to show there exists some
\tilde{b}
such that

\left\lVert\left\langle U_{i},\,t\right\rangle\right\rVert_{\psi_{2}}\leq C\tilde{b}\left\langle t,\,\Sigma t\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}.

It would suffice to argue
T_{\lambda}^{-1}TT_{\lambda}^{-1}\preceq\Sigma
. By Lemma
G.8
,

T_{\lambda}^{-1}TT_{\lambda}^{-1}\preceq\frac{1}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}}\Sigma

and hence

\displaystyle\left\lVert\left\langle U_{i},\,t\right\rangle\right\rVert_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\kappa\left\lVert f_{0}\right\rVert+\bar{\sigma})\frac{1}{\underline{\sigma}}b\left\langle t,\,\Sigma t\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}

i.e.
\tilde{b}=\frac{(\kappa\left\lVert f_{0}\right\rVert+\bar{\sigma})}{\underline{\sigma}}b.

∎

G.4
Bounding (Q,R,L,B)

G.4.1
Bounding L

First, we lower bound the size of the Gaussian process
Z
(with covariance
\Sigma
) that approximates the deviations of the estimator.

Lemma G.13

(Variance lower bound)
.

Let
Z
be a Gaussian random element of
H
with covariance
\Sigma
, and suppose
\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_{i}^{2}|X_{i}]\geq\underline{\sigma}^{2}
almost surely. Put
M\coloneqq\kappa^{2}\left\lVert f_{0}\right\rVert^{2}+\bar{\sigma}^{2}
. Then with probability at least
1-\eta
,

\left\lVert Z\right\rVert\geq L(\lambda,\eta):=\sqrt{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}-\Big\{2+\sqrt{2\log(1/\eta)}\Big\}\sqrt{M/\lambda}.

Proof of Lemma
G.13
.

We will lower bound
\mathbb{E}\lVert Z\rVert
using the identity,

\{\mathbb{E}(\lVert Z\rVert)\}^{2}=\mathbb{E}(\lVert Z\rVert^{2})-\mathbb{E}\{\lVert Z\rVert-\mathbb{E}(\lVert Z\rVert)\}^{2},

(20)

by bounding both terms on the right-hand side. Finally we will apply Gaussian concentration for a high-probability bound.

1.

Upper bounding
\mathbb{E}(\lVert Z\rVert-\mathbb{E}\lVert Z\rVert)^{2}
. Firstly, recall that
\left\lVert Z\right\rVert
is the supremum of the Gaussian process
\left\langle Z,\,t\right\rangle
indexed by
T=\left\{t\in H\,\middle|\,\left\lVert t\right\rVert\leq 1\right\}
. Moreover, we have
\sigma^{2}_{T}\coloneqq\sup_{t\in T}\mathbb{E}\left\langle Z,\,t\right\rangle^{2}=\left\lVert\Sigma\right\rVert_{op}.

By Lemma
G.7
,

\Sigma\preceq MT_{\lambda}^{-2}T,

it follows that

\sigma^{2}_{T}=\left\lVert\Sigma\right\rVert_{op}\leq M\left\lVert T_{\lambda}^{-2}T\right\rVert_{op}\leq M/\lambda,

where the bound
\lVert T_{\lambda}^{-2}T\rVert_{op}\leq 1/\lambda
follows by maximizing
s\mapsto(s+\lambda)^{-2}s
. Similarly,

\mathbb{E}\left\lVert Z\right\rVert^{2}=\tr\Sigma\leq M\tr(T_{\lambda}^{-2}T)=M\mathfrak{n}(\lambda),

so by Markov’s inequality
\left\langle Z,\,t\right\rangle
is a.s. bounded on
T
. Thus, by combining the two inequalities of Lemma
D.4
with a union bound we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{(\left\lVert Z\right\rVert-\mathbb{E}\left\lVert Z\right\rVert)^{2}\geq u\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left\{|\left\lVert Z\right\rVert-\mathbb{E}\left\lVert Z\right\rVert|\geq\sqrt{u}\right\}\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{u}{2\lVert\Sigma\rVert_{op}}\right).

By integrating the tail,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(\left\lVert Z\right\rVert-\mathbb{E}\left\lVert Z\right\rVert)^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\left\{(\left\lVert Z\right\rVert-\mathbb{E}\left\lVert Z\right\rVert)^{2}\geq u\right\}\,du

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}2\exp\left(-\frac{u}{2\lVert\Sigma\rVert_{op}}\right)\,du

\displaystyle=4\lVert\Sigma\rVert_{op}

\displaystyle\leq 4M/\lambda.

2.

Lower bounding
\mathbb{E}(\lVert Z\rVert^{2})
. Similarly using the upper bound
\Sigma\succeq\underline{\sigma}^{2}T_{\lambda}^{-2}T
from Lemma
G.8
, we find that

\mathbb{E}\lVert Z\rVert^{2}=\tr\Sigma\geq\underline{\sigma}^{2}\tr(T_{\lambda}^{-2}T)=\underline{\sigma}^{2}\mathfrak{n}(\lambda).

3.

Combining. Combining these estimates in (
20
), we have

\mathbb{E}\lVert Z\rVert\geq\sqrt{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)-4M/\lambda}.

By a final application of the lower tail bound in Lemma
D.4
(in inverted form) we have that w.p.
1-\eta
,

\left\lVert Z\right\rVert\geq\mathbb{E}\lVert Z\rVert-\sqrt{2\lVert\Sigma\rVert_{op}\log(1/\eta)}\geq\sqrt{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)-4M/\lambda}-\sqrt{2M\log(1/\eta)/\lambda}.

Since
\sqrt{a-b}\geq\sqrt{a}-\sqrt{b}
(rearrange and square both sides), this simplifies to

\left\lVert Z\right\rVert\geq\sqrt{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}-\Big\{2+\sqrt{2\log(1/\eta)}\Big\}\sqrt{M/\lambda}.

∎

G.4.2
Q and R for bounded summands

In what follows, define
M:=\kappa^{2}\|f_{0}\|+\bar{\sigma}\kappa
.

Proposition G.14

(Gaussian approximation: Bounded)
.

Suppose Assumption
6.10
holds. Then there exists a sequence
(Z_{i})_{1\leq i\leq n}
of Gaussian random elements in
H
, with covariance
\Sigma
, such that with probability
1-\eta
,

\left\lVert\sqrt{n}(\hat{f}-f_{\lambda})-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{i}\right\rVert\lesssim Q_{\mathrm{bd}}(T,n,\lambda)M\log(12/\eta)+\frac{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}{\sqrt{n}}M\log(8/\eta)^{2},

where

Q_{\mathrm{bd}}(T,n,\lambda)=\frac{1}{\lambda}\inf_{m\geq 1}\left\{\sigma(T,m)+\frac{m^{2}\log(m^{2})}{\sqrt{n}}\right\}.

Proof.

By Theorem
B.4
, with probability at least
1-\eta
,

\left\lVert\sqrt{n}(\hat{f}-f_{\lambda})-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}U_{i}\right\rVert\lesssim\frac{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}{\sqrt{n}}M\log(4/\eta)^{2}

since

\frac{2\kappa}{n\lambda}\leq\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}{n}}\iff n\geq\frac{4\kappa^{2}}{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)\lambda^{2}}.

Then, using Proposition
C.7
together with the bounds deduced in Lemmas
G.10
and
G.11
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(U_{i}-Z_{i})\right\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\inf_{m\geq 1}\left\{\sqrt{\log(6/\eta)}\sigma(\Sigma,m)+\frac{am^{2}\log(m^{2}/\eta)}{\sqrt{n}}\right\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\inf_{m\geq 1}\left\{\frac{M\sigma(T,m)}{\lambda}\sqrt{\log(6/\eta)}+m^{2}\frac{M\log(m^{2}/\eta)}{\lambda\sqrt{n}}\right\}

holds with probability at least
1-\eta
.
Thus, by a union bound, it holds with probability
1-\eta
that

\left\lVert\sqrt{n}(\hat{f}-f_{\lambda})-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{i}\right\rVert\lesssim Q_{\mathrm{bd}}(T,n,\lambda)M\log(12/\eta)+\frac{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}{\sqrt{n}}M\log(8/\eta)^{2},

where we have simplified and consolidated log factors.
∎

Recall the bootstrap process

\mathfrak{B}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}h_{ij}\left(\frac{\hat{V}_{i}-\hat{V}_{j}}{\sqrt{2}}\right),\quad\hat{V}_{i}=\{Y_{i}-\hat{f}(X_{i})\}\hat{T}_{\lambda}^{-1}k_{X_{i}}.

Proposition G.15

(Bootstrap approximation: Bounded)
.

Suppose Assumption
6.10
holds. Then, there exists a random variable
Z
whose conditional distribution given
U
is Gaussian with covariance
\Sigma
, and such that with probability at least
1-\eta

\mathbb{P}\left[\vphantom{\sum}\left\lVert\mathfrak{B}-Z\right\rVert\lesssim M\log(16/\eta)^{2}\left\{R_{\mathrm{bd}}(T,n,\lambda)+\frac{\kappa\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}{\sqrt{n}}\right\}\middle|U\right]\geq 1-\eta

where

R_{\mathrm{bd}}(T,n,\lambda)\coloneqq\inf_{m\geq 1}\left\{\left(\frac{m\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}{\lambda^{2}n}+\frac{m}{\lambda^{4}n^{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}+\frac{1}{\lambda}\sigma(T,m)\right\}.

Proof.

Under Assumption
6.10
, Theorem
E.9
implies that

\left\|\mathfrak{B}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}h_{ij}\frac{U_{i}-U_{j}}{\sqrt{2}}\right\|\lesssim\frac{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}{\sqrt{n}}\kappa M\ln(16/\eta)^{2}

holds with probability at least
1-\eta
since
n\geq\frac{4\kappa^{2}}{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)\lambda^{2}}
implies

\frac{2\kappa}{n\lambda}\leq\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}{n}},\quad\sqrt{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}\lambda},\quad\sqrt{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}\geq\frac{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)^{1/4}}{n^{1/4}\lambda^{1/2}}.

We use Corollary
D.15
along with the bounds in Lemmas
G.10
and
G.11
. In particular, set
W=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}h_{ij}\frac{U_{i}-U_{j}}{\sqrt{2}}
,
W^{\prime}=\mathfrak{B}
, and
\delta=\frac{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}{\sqrt{n}}\kappa M\ln(16/\eta)^{2}
. Then there must exist
Z
with the desired conditional distribution, such that with probability at least
1-\eta
, the
\sigma(U)
-conditional probability of the event

\displaystyle\left\lVert Z-\mathfrak{B}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\lesssim\log(6/\eta)^{3/2}\inf_{m\geq 1}\bigg\{m^{\frac{1}{4}}\left(\frac{M^{2}\cdot M^{2}\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}{\lambda^{2}n}+\frac{M^{4}}{\lambda^{4}n^{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{M}{\lambda}\sigma(T,m)\bigg\}+\frac{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}{\sqrt{n}}\kappa M\log(16/\eta)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim M\log(16/\eta)^{2}\left(\inf_{m\geq 1}\left\{m^{\frac{1}{4}}\left(\frac{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}{\lambda^{2}n}+\frac{1}{\lambda^{4}n^{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}+\frac{\sigma(T,m)}{\lambda}\right\}+\frac{\kappa\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}{\sqrt{n}}\right)

\displaystyle=M\log(16/\eta)^{2}\left(R_{\mathrm{bd}}(T,n,\lambda)+\frac{\kappa\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}{\sqrt{n}}\right)

is at least
1-\eta
when
n\geq 2
.
∎

G.4.3
Q and R for sub-Gaussian summands

In what follows, define
\tilde{M}:=\frac{1}{\underline{\sigma}}(\kappa\|f_{0}\|+\bar{\sigma})
and
\bar{M}=M\vee\tilde{M}
.

Proposition G.16

(Gaussian approximation: Sub-Gaussian)
.

Suppose Assumptions
6.10
and
6.12
hold. Then there exists a Gaussian random element in
H
, with covariance
\Sigma
, such that with probability
1-\eta
,

\left\lVert\sqrt{n}(\hat{f}-f_{\lambda})-Z\right\rVert\lesssim Q_{\mathrm{sg}}(T,n,\lambda)\bar{M}^{4}b^{3}(6/\eta)^{1/\log(mn)}+\frac{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}{\sqrt{n}}M\log(8/\eta)^{2},

where

Q_{\mathrm{sg}}(T,n,\lambda)=\inf_{m\geq 1}\left\{\frac{\sigma(T,m)}{\lambda}+\frac{m^{3/2}\log(n^{2})}{\sqrt{n\lambda}}\right\}.

Proof.

By Theorem
B.4
, with probability at least
1-\eta
,

\left\lVert\sqrt{n}(\hat{f}-f_{\lambda})-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}U_{i}\right\rVert\lesssim\frac{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}{\sqrt{n}}M\log(4/\eta)^{2}

since

\frac{2\kappa}{n\lambda}\leq\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}{n}}\iff n\geq\frac{4\kappa^{2}}{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)\lambda^{2}}.

Then, using Proposition
C.8
together with the bounds deduced in Lemmas
G.10
and
G.12
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}U_{i}-Z\right\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\inf_{m\geq 1}\left\{\tilde{b}\sigma(\Sigma,m)\sqrt{\log(6/\eta)}+\left\lVert\Sigma\right\rVert_{op}^{\frac{1}{2}}\tilde{b}^{3}\left(\frac{m^{\frac{3}{2}}\log(n)^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)(3/\eta)^{1/\log(mn)}\right\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\inf_{m\geq 1}\left\{\tilde{M}b\frac{M\sigma(T,m)}{\lambda}\sqrt{\log(6/\eta)}+\frac{M}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\tilde{M}^{3}b^{3}\left(\frac{m^{\frac{3}{2}}\log(n)^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)(3/\eta)^{1/\log(mn)}\right\}

holds with probability at least
1-\eta
. Note that we use the bound

\|\Sigma\|_{op}\leq M^{2}\|T_{\lambda}^{-2}T\|_{op}\leq M^{2}\frac{1}{\lambda}

which is justified in the proof of Proposition
I.4
with
s=1
and
c=2
.

Thus, by a union bound, it holds with probability
1-\eta
that

\left\lVert\sqrt{n}(\hat{f}-f_{\lambda})-Z\right\rVert\lesssim Q_{\mathrm{bd}}(T,n,\lambda)\bar{M}^{4}b^{3}(6/\eta)^{1/\log(mn)}+\frac{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}{\sqrt{n}}M\log(8/\eta)^{2},

where we have simplified and consolidated log factors.
∎

Proposition G.17

(Bootstrap approximation: Sub-Gaussian)
.

Suppose Assumptions
6.10
and
6.12
hold. Then, there exists a random variable
Z
whose conditional distribution given
U
is Gaussian with covariance
\Sigma
, and such that with probability at least
1-\eta

\mathbb{P}\left[\vphantom{\sum}\left\lVert\mathfrak{B}-Z\right\rVert\lesssim\bar{M}^{2}b\cdot\log(16/\eta)^{2}\left\{R_{\mathrm{sg}}(T,n,\lambda)+\frac{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}{\sqrt{n}}\right\}\middle|U\right]\geq 1-\eta

where

R_{\mathrm{sg}}(T,n,\lambda)\coloneqq\inf_{m\geq 1}\left\{\left(\frac{m\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)^{2}}{n}\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}+\frac{1}{\lambda}\sigma(T,m)\right\}.

Proof.

Under Assumption
6.10
, Theorem
E.9
implies that

\left\|\mathfrak{B}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}h_{ij}\frac{U_{i}-U_{j}}{\sqrt{2}}\right\|\lesssim\frac{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}{\sqrt{n}}\kappa M\ln(16/\eta)^{2}

holds with probability at least
1-\eta
since
n\geq\frac{4\kappa^{2}}{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)\lambda^{2}}
implies

\frac{2\kappa}{n\lambda}\leq\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}{n}},\quad\sqrt{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}\lambda},\quad\sqrt{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}\geq\frac{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)^{1/4}}{n^{1/4}\lambda^{1/2}}.

We use Corollary
D.15
along with the bounds in Lemmas
G.10
and
G.12
. In particular, set
W=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}h_{ij}\frac{U_{i}-U_{j}}{\sqrt{2}}
,
W^{\prime}=\mathfrak{B}
, and
\delta=\frac{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}{\sqrt{n}}\kappa M\ln(16/\eta)^{2}
. Then there must exist
Z
with the desired conditional distribution, such that with probability at least
1-\eta
, the
\sigma(U)
-conditional probability of the event

\displaystyle\left\lVert Z-\mathfrak{B}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\lesssim\log(6/\eta)^{3/2}\inf_{m\geq 1}\left\{\left(\frac{m}{n}\tilde{M}^{4}b^{4}M^{4}\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}+\frac{M}{\lambda}\sigma(T,m)\right\}+\frac{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}{\sqrt{n}}\kappa M\log(16/\eta)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bar{M}^{2}b\cdot\log(16/\eta)^{2}\left(\inf_{m\geq 1}\left\{\left(\frac{m\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)^{2}}{n}\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}+\frac{\sigma(T,m)}{\lambda}\right\}+\frac{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}{\sqrt{n}}\right)

\displaystyle=\bar{M}^{2}b\cdot\log(16/\eta)^{2}\left(R_{\mathrm{sg}}(T,n,\lambda)+\frac{\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}{\sqrt{n}}\right)

is at least
1-\eta
.
∎

G.5
Corollaries

Recall the definitions of quantities appearing in Theorem
G.6
:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} Q_{\mathrm{bd}}(n,\lambda)&=\frac{1}{\lambda}\inf_{m\geq 1}\left\{\sigma(m)+\frac{m^{2}\log(m^{2})}{\sqrt{n}}\right\},\\
R_{\mathrm{bd}}(n,\lambda)&=\inf_{m\geq 1}\left[\left\{\frac{m\,\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}{\lambda^{2}n}+\frac{m}{\lambda^{4}n^{2}}\right\}^{\frac{1}{4}}+\frac{\sigma(m)}{\lambda}\right],\\
\end{aligned}\quad\begin{aligned} Q_{\mathrm{sg}}(n,\lambda)&=\inf_{m\geq 1}\left\{\frac{\sigma(m)}{\lambda}+\frac{m^{\frac{3}{2}}\log(n)^{2}}{\sqrt{n\lambda}}\right\},\\
R_{\mathrm{sg}}(n,\lambda)&=\inf_{m\geq 1}\left[\left\{\frac{m\,\mathfrak{n}^{2}(\lambda)}{n}\right\}^{\frac{1}{4}}+\frac{\sigma(m)}{\lambda}\right].\\
\end{aligned}

We will now give a simplified asymptotic characterization for each of these expressions under two different assumptions on the spectrum of
T
, namely
(i) polynomial decay, i.e.
\nu_{s}(T)\leq\omega s^{-\beta}
;
(ii) exponential decay, i.e.
\nu_{s}(T)\leq\omega\exp(-\alpha s^{\gamma})
.
We suppress dependence on
T
in the notation. Note that, within
R_{\mathrm{bd}}
,

\frac{m\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}{\lambda^{2}n}\geq\frac{m}{\lambda^{4}n^{2}}\iff n\geq\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)}

which is implied by Assumption
6.10
.

Sobolev RKHS: Bounded data

We proceed in steps.

1.

If
\nu_{s}\leq\omega s^{-\beta}
then by Proposition
I.2
we have
\sigma(m)\lesssim_{\beta,\omega}m^{1/2-\beta/2}
, and by Proposition
I.4
we have
\mathfrak{n}(\lambda)\lesssim_{\beta,\omega}\lambda^{-1-1/\beta}
.

2.

First we study
Q_{\mathrm{bd}}(n,\lambda)
. Plugging in our bound on
\sigma(m)
and then equating the main terms (ignoring constants and log factors) gives

m^{1/2-\beta/2}=\frac{m^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\iff m=n^{1/(3+\beta)}.

Plugging this in and simplifying exponents in the logarithm gives

\displaystyle Q_{\mathrm{bd}}(n,\lambda)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\beta,\omega}\lambda^{-1}\cdot(n^{\frac{1}{3+\beta}})^{\frac{1-\beta}{2}}\cdot\log(n)

\displaystyle=\lambda^{-1}n^{(1-\beta)/(6+2\beta)}\log(n)

\displaystyle=\log(n)\left(\frac{1}{n\lambda^{\frac{6+2\beta}{\beta-1}}}\right)^{(\beta-1)/(6+2\beta)}.

3.

Next we study
R_{\mathrm{bd}}(n,\lambda)
. Plugging in our bounds and equating main terms gives

\displaystyle m^{\frac{1}{4}}\left\{\frac{\lambda^{-1-1/\beta}}{\lambda^{2}n}\right\}^{\frac{1}{4}}

\displaystyle=\frac{m^{1/2-\beta/2}}{\lambda}

\displaystyle\iff\left\{\frac{\lambda^{1-1/\beta}}{n}\right\}^{\frac{1}{1-2\beta}}

\displaystyle=m.

Using this value of
m
gives the bound

\displaystyle R_{\mathrm{bd}}(n,\lambda)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\beta,\omega}\left\{\frac{m^{1/2-\beta/2}}{\lambda}\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\lambda}\left\{\frac{\lambda^{1-1/\beta}}{n}\right\}^{\frac{1-\beta}{2-4\beta}}
